What a finished driver needs (2026-10-10)

What a finished driver needs and what is open: the work items (P, S, V, A, M, R) and one row per missing piece (N1 onward, a card each with a status badge, searchable and sortable). Agents add and update rows here.

  2. State per area (updated 2026-10-14, card run 56)

  7. Done 2026-10-12 (sim / host verified, CI on the self-hosted runners; card: step big4)
  8. Order
  9. D4: display state across resets and sleep (2026-10-10, sim/host verified, step sleep)

The roadmap from today's state to a finished driver: what exists, what is missing, what can be written now without the card, and what only a card run can settle. docs/PLAN-NEXT.html stays the log of the items already done; this file decides the order from here.

Two finish lines

D: desktop driverM: Metal driver
What the user getsmacOS on the RX 7800 XT as the only GPU: both monitors, every resolution the monitor offers, hotplug, sleep/wake, idle power near Linux (~12-15 W), stable for daysD + Metal apps run on the GPU (Safari, Preview, Final Cut, games through Metal/MoltenVK), WindowServer composites on the GPU
Left (estimate)~30-40 %~70-80 %
Biggest riskDCN mode setting without the full amdgpu DC/DMLthe Metal plugin (NullMoth port) has never run here; how much of Metal it covers decides everything

Horizon: macOS 26 is the last x86_64 release; the work only pays while it is supported.

State per area (updated 2026-10-14, card run 56)

Legend: card = verified on the card (docs/status/hw-verified.txt), sim = simulator/host tests only, - = missing. The amdgpu functions that apply to this card are all ported (docs/status/STATUS.html: 1483 of 1483); what the table lists as sim waits for its card step.

Video (VCN 4.0)-VCN 4.0 + JPEG 4.0 rings, VIDEO_CAPS and CS, DPG power gating, suspend/resumethe card run (steps vcn, vcn2, vcnsleep); decode/encode from Mesa; VideoToolbox (optional)

Work items

Size: S < 300 lines, M 300-1500, L 1500-5000, XL > 5000 (or unknown). now = can be written and verified on the host/simulator today; 1 boot = written now, one card run confirms (batched into the round's single big step); iterate = depends on what the card / macOS does, needs several rounds, cannot be written far ahead.

P - power (finish line D)

#Itemamdgpu sourceSizeTest
P1Non-GFX clock gating: MMHUB, ATHUB, HDP, BIF/NBIO, IH, SDMA, MP0/MP1 light sleepsoc21_common_set_clockgating_state, mmhub_v3_0_set_clockgating, athub_v3_0_set_clockgating, hdp_v6_0_set_clockgating_state, nbio_v4_3_update_medium_grain_*, sdma_v6_0_set_clockgating_stateMnow -> 1 boot (watts in the PM trend)
P2Why the PMFW counts GFX busy: compare every register amdgpu writes after gfx_v11_0_hw_init that we skip (RLC CGTT, RLC_PG_*, IMU, CP_*_IDLE), log them; GFXOFF entry/exit counters (smu_v13_0_0_get_gfx_off_residency)gfx_v11_0_set_powergating_state, gfx_v11_0_update_gfx_clock_gating, amdgpu_gfx_off_ctrlMnow (diagnostics) -> iterate
P3Power profile + fan as amdgpu's defaults (BOOTUP_DEFAULT workload, fan curve from the PPTable), rdna3pm=2 defaultsmu_v13_0_0_set_power_profile_modeS1 boot
P4Suspend/resume (S3) and shutdown: setPowerState -> the reinit path the reset already has (fini in reverse, VRAM kept, rings re-created, display restored)amdgpu_device_suspend / _resume, amdgpu_device_ip_suspend_phase1/2Lnow (sim: suspend+resume cycle) -> iterate (VM sleep with passthrough is unreliable; bare metal)
P5Runtime idle: no PreventSystemIdleSleep assertion once P4 works-Swith P4

S - display (finish line D)

#Itemamdgpu sourceSizeTest
S1Mode list from the EDID (detailed timings + CTA/DisplayID), filtered to what the link/clock can carrydrm_add_edid_modes, dc_link_validate_modeMnow
S2Own mode set on a lit TMDS head: OTG timing, pixel clock (DCCG DTO / VBIOS SetPixelClock / DMCUB), HUBP surface, OPP/MPC, encoderdcn20_enable_stream_timing, dcn20_program_pipe, dce110_enable_stream, optc1_program_timingLnow (sim model) -> iterate (a wrong value = black screen; the boot step tries the variant table and keeps the GOP mode as fallback)
S3Bandwidth: DISPCLK/DPPCLK/DCFCLK minima through the SMU, watermarks/DLG for the modes S1 allows (a table for <= 4K60, not full DML)dcn32_prepare_bandwidth, dcn32_calculate_wm_and_dlgLnow -> 1 boot per mode class
S4Framebuffer: getDisplayModes/setDisplayMode -> S2, connect interrupt on HPD (S7 of the old plan done in sim), one fb per head alwaysIOFramebufferM1 boot (the report switches modes itself and grabs pictures)
S5Gamma/regamma LUT (setGammaTable, Night Shift, calibration)dpp3_program_blnd_lut, dpp20_program_regammaMnow -> 1 boot
S6DPMS: blank/unblank and display sleepdcn10_blank_pixel_data, dce110_blank_streamSnow -> 1 boot
S7DP SST link training (native DP monitors)dp_perform_link_training, dpcdLiterate (needs a native DP monitor)
S8HDMI/DP audiodce_audLlater (optional)

V - Vulkan (feeds M)

#ItemSizeTest
V1rdna3_vktest: draw a triangle into an image and read it back, image layout/format copies, timeline semaphores, two queues at onceMnow (host loopback runs the submit path) -> 1 boot
V2CTS subset in the report (deqp-vk with a fixed case list: api., memory., compute.*, a few draw cases), results summarised by triageM1 boot per round (runs unattended)
V3Present: VK_EXT_metal_surface comes from MoltenVK's world; for RADV on macOS: present through the accelerator (IOSurface -> flip)Literate

A - accelerator / WindowServer (D, prerequisite of M)

#ItemSizeTest
A1Two heads confirmed by the scanout pictures, then rdna3accel=1 rdna3accelfb=4 rdna3acceliop=1 default (boot-loop guard kept: -rdna3off)S1 boot
A2Swap copies by SDMA/GPU instead of the CPU (submitSwapCopy -> SDMA linear copy), BAR reads avoidedMnow -> 1 boot
A3IOSurface <-> GEM BO (same VRAM allocation visible to WindowServer and to RADV/Metal): RD_NR_XNU_SURFACE_BO gives a BO over the accelerator's allocation for a surface plane (looked up in the caller's task, referenced while the BO lives); libdrm_xnu's drm_xnu_surface_fd makes it a dma-buf fd for RADV's OPAQUE_FD import, the Metal plugin uses it where NVK used its RM hook. Card check: plugin log "item 7: ... live in the surface's family VRAM", kernel log "GEM: IOSurface ... (A3)". Open question for the card: if the accelerator ever re-backs a surface (deallocPhysical, then a new allocation) while a BO holds the old range, RADV would draw where WindowServer no longer reads - stale content, not corruption. Security (ECC review): isolation rests on IOSurfaceRoot's lookup in the caller's task, checked by rdna3_drminfo --surface-xproc in every report (SURFXPROC: another process's surface must give ENOENT); surface BOs cannot be exported, one file pins at most half the visible VRAM; still open: DIRTY is not tied to a surface the file imported (any process may mark a surface it can look up). Card run 58 (2026-10-15c, first card run): every process shared surfaces, but WindowServer's display framebuffers (the 1920x1080 compositing surfaces) got BOs over a second VRAM copy that no swap scans out (the legacy surface's buffers are other allocations filled by the family's page-on): the main screen kept an old frame. They are kept out of A3 now (common/rdna3_a3_display.h, rdna3a3display=1 lets them in, N27)Lcard run 58: works for app surfaces; display surfaces kept out (N27)

#ItemSizeTest
M1Install the plugin + translator + RADV into /Library/GPUBundles/rdna3mtl from rdna3vm prepare, debug.rdna3accelfb=1 by boot-arg; report runs mtlprobe (MTLCopyAllDevices, device name, families)M1 boot
M2Compute: a Metal compute kernel (shipped as a precompiled .metallib, built in CI on the macOS runner) through translator -> RADV, result checkedMiterate
M3Render pass offscreen, texture formats, blitsLiterate
M4CAMetalLayer / drawable present (IOSurface from A3, flip)Literate
M5WindowServer on Metal (SkyLight's Metal compositor instead of the CPU path)XLiterate
M6App coverage (Safari, Preview, a game), allow-list widenedXLiterate

R - robustness and release

#ItemSizeTest
R1Hang/recovery on the card: the report submits a deliberately hanging IB and checks the soft reset pathS1 boot
R2Soak: the report loops vktest/DMA for N minutes, PM trend, no leaks (ioreg BO counts)S1 boot (longer step)
R3Bare metal: docs/TESTING-HW.html run on the real machine (GOP cold boot, no Linux before)-user, once D is near
R4Tahoe (macOS 26): NM_TAHOE=1 accelerator layout, kext checksMVM with 26
R5Release: signed-as-possible bundle, package/ installer, OpenCore snippet, versioned artifactsMnow
R6Video VCN 4.0 + VideoToolboxXLoptional, last

N - not built at all (inventory 2026-10-15)

Three code-explorer agents went through the kernel, accel/metal and the userspace/installer for what does not exist yet (not what is untested). Order is by what a user misses most; "quick" = the first batch. Status: open / in work / done (with the commit).

N24 open

VMIDs held for a process's whole life: one of the 15 hardware VMIDs (7 with rdna3userq) per open DRM file, so with the Metal plugin serving every app (N1) the card runs out after about a dozen programs and the next one gets no GPU. amdgpu binds a VMID only while a job runs and takes it back when the job's fence signals (amdgpu_vmid_grab, amdgpu_vmid_grab_idle, amdgpu_vmid_grab_reserved, the VMID manager amdgpu_vmid_mgr_init, the page-table flush on a reused VMID amdgpu_vm_flush); port that so VMIDs are shared in time. Until then the open is refused cleanly when none is free, at most 8 per uid, the last 2 for root and _windowserver (N18); residuals (ECC security review): one app of the console user can take that uid's share and lock the user's other GPU apps out; after a fast user switch the previous console user keeps its VMIDs and the new one may get none

Where it shows
kext, hw/gpuvm
Size
L
Status
open
N25 open

GPU hang recovery is triggered by whoever waits 10 s on a fence (drmFenceWait -> recoverHang), not by how long the job itself has run: a process can still make the kernel reset another process's long job by submitting a NOP behind it and waiting. Port amdgpu's job timeout (drm_sched job timeout per ring, amdgpu_job_timedout), so recovery follows the running job's own runtime

Where it shows
kext, uapi
Size
M
Status
open
N26 open

Card run 57: the HDMI boot head's monitor said "no signal" while its OTG ran with the desktop (DP clone fine); driver log identical to run 56, where both worked. Diagnostic build: the TX watch (hw/dcn32_txstate.c, RDNA3,Head<n>,TX, the looks' TX lines). Next run: the TX at the start / after the clone / TX change lines and the look's TX problems; when the monitor went dark (from the boot screen, at WindowServer, after a test step). Latent, rdna3vrr=1 only: optc32_set_drr without bounds selects V_TOTAL_MIN / MAX, which on a head the driver did not mode-set may be 0 (the TX watch names it DRR bound 0; displayVrrCommit runs only after a mode set, which programs them)

N27 partly done

Card run 58 (2026-10-15c): the main screen kept an old frame (wallpaper and menu bar) while WindowServer's windows flashed in and out; root cause: A3 BOs over WindowServer's display framebuffers, a VRAM copy no swap scans out (flips only ever went to the legacy surface's buffers 0xb100000 / 0xc0e0000 / 0xc8d0000, the BOs sat at 0xd0f0000..0xe8d0000). Interim (done): those surfaces stay out of A3, composited through copies (rdna3a3display=1 / debug.rdna3accel_a3display=1 restores the run 58 behaviour; off while debug.rdna3accel_iop_flip=1); a display surface is a WindowServer surface of the size a framebuffer scans out at the import (asked live) or of the head's own swaps; the kernel knows the importing process only, not the creator, so WindowServer's import of an application's full-screen surface is refused too and read through copies (not counted apart: IOSurface exposes no creator to the kernel); a3 display: lines, debug.rdna3accel_swaps_h<N> / swaps_a3_h<N>, triage A3 display and M6 screen (a pipe frozen through the M6 looks). Open: (1) zero-copy for the display: map a legacy swap buffer to its IOSurface and flip the IOSurface's own VRAM when the dirty mark says it is newest, then let display surfaces back in; (2) VRAM is not cleared on allocation (the new WindowServer's buffer at 0xbc50000 showed the old WindowServer's last frame); (3) Terminal (apps mode) TCP reads at an unmapped VA 0x80018223f000 during and after the 960x540 mode switch, 9 times, still after its VM was marked guilty, each followed by 4 VMID 0 CPC writes at address 0 (the 45 VMFaults); WindowServer's GFX job hung at the same mode switch (soft reset, WindowServer restarted) ; card run 58b (2026-10-15d): with the display surfaces out, windows and menus flickered on both screens - WindowServer's 615 other inputs were A3 BOs it also wrote (747 surface-in uploads into shared VRAM) while their owners drew there; every WindowServer import is now kept out by default (rdna3a3ws=1 / rdna3a3=0), A3 stays for applications' own and shared surfaces; (4) open: an ordering of owners' GPU / CPU writes with WindowServer's reads (dirty marks the reader honours, no reader uploads into shared VRAM) before WindowServer's inputs can join A3; the kernel's A B A B flicker counters say whether the copy path flickers too; (5) open: flicker confined to one band of one head (a small window or one status item) reads like a blinking caret and is only a WARN (swaps_ababwide_h<N> counts the swaps where more than one band alternated); the 8 bands x 48 columns sample can miss a small item altogether; card run 58b's second run (2026-10-16): the flicker stayed with every WindowServer input kept out - its cause was copy forward copying nothing while two back buffers took turns (fixed, common/rdna3_swap_front.h), so whether WindowServer's inputs can go back into A3 is open again once the flicker is gone; (6) open after the ECC review of 60526e4: a copy forward that missed rects keeps them pending for that buffer (8 rects, then the whole picture) and copies them with the next copy forward into it from a source that did not miss them itself (after 3 calls blocked by a source that missed them too, copied anyway: both backs then hold the same stale region until WindowServer redraws it), not reported to CoreDisplay (which would switch copy forward off for good, card run 54) - if the card shows rects stale for long, reporting after a run of misses is the alternative; the record copy forward reads is the buffer the head's last swap presented even when the flip failed (WindowServer's frames, not the panel's); the IOSurface IOP flips (rdna3accel_iop_flip=1) and the legacy swaps share the per-head record, a legacy copy forward then finds a foreign buffer and copies nothing

N28 partly done

Card run 2026-10-17 (~10th run of "windows and menus flicker on both screens": a window shows, then the wallpaper where it was): copy forward works now (h1 3080 from the shown buffer, 0 missed) and the A B A B test sees little (1 % / 15 %), but it hashes at swap time only. Code finding: rd3AccelFlipTo -> flipToLocked programs the HUBP and returns, the flip latches at the next vblank, so submitSwapCopy gave WindowServer the old buffer back while the panel still read it (H1). Diagnostic build + fix (common/rdna3_flip_latch.h, tests/test_flip_latch.c): the swap waits for the latch (debug.rdna3accel_swap_latch / boot-arg rdna3swaplatch, default 1; amdgpu: the flip event from dm_pflip_high_irq), the counters swaps_latchpend/_latchwait/_latchus/_latchmax/_latchtimeout/_latchunknown/_latchoff_h<N>, cf_onpanel_h<N>, the torn test (swaps_tornchk/_torn/_inplace/_tornskip_h<N>, debug.rdna3accel_swap_torn), swaps_shownfreed_h<N>, swaps_emptyfilled/_emptystill_h<N> (triage flip latch). H2 (the flip before the GPU finished the frame) has no mechanism in the default configuration: the legacy buffers are written only by the family's page-on (CPU) and copy forward (SDMA, waited for), WindowServer's GPU composite reaches them through the plugin's surface-out after vkQueueSubmit was waited for; no GPU-wait sysctl was added (swaps_a3_h<N> counts flips of GPU-written A3 BOs, 0 with rdna3a3display=0). Open: (1) with one back buffer the zero-copy flip shows the buffer WindowServer draws into (swaps_inplace_h<N>): a copy into the front (what submitSwapCopy asks for) or a third buffer if the card shows it; (2) the latch wait sleeps in the swap under the family's lock (the family hands the old buffer back when submitSwapCopy returns), up to two OTG frames or the cap (2.5 frames, 35-100 ms) per swap, and two heads may take turns there and halve the frame rate (A/B with swap_latch=0, the card run measures it); the alternative is completing the swap's IOAccelEvent (submitSwapCopy's a0) from the flip-done instead of waiting (a later redesign); a swap waits once, after its own flip, measured from that flip (2.5 of the head's frames from its measured refresh, 35-100 ms; the next swap's entry only looks: review round 2 removed the entry wait, which a shared budget made unable to time out a stuck flip), 3 timeouts in a row turn a head's waits off until a flip is seen latched (logged both ways, swaps_latchwaitsoff_h<N>); with swap_latch=0 the feature sleeps nowhere (the empty-back re-read too); ECC review round 1 fixed the backoff that never recovered, the entry wait ignoring swap_latch=0, the 60 Hz-only cap, the silent status, hash and flip failures and triage for archive-only reports; (3) a buffer freed while scanned (swaps_shownfreed_h<N>) is only counted; (4) the 320 of 1030 skipped swaps on head 0 (empty back): swaps_emptyfilled_h<N> says whether the frame lands late; (5) WindowServer crashed after the mode switch (plugin fence wait 5000 ms, GPU hang) and Terminal's VMID 5 TCP read faults stay open (N27 (3)); (6) the IOP flips (rdna3accel_iop_flip=1) do not update the latch record

Where it shows
accel, triage
Size
M
Status
diagnostic + fix, card check open
N29 superseded

Card run 2026-10-17b (both screens mostly black, content now and then, everything flickers; the flip latch from N28 works: 0 pending, 0 timeouts): head 0's swaps name the framebuffer resource as the front and two backs in turns; the second back read empty at every swap and the family's aperture access (page-on) never named it; 85 of 229 shown frames rewritten in place; 14 + 10 buffers freed while scanned; at the mode switch the core refused VRAM (0xe00002db, the CPU-visible heap) and CoreDisplay switched copy forward off (0xe00002bc: the family's, the driver answers only success). Root model mismatch (H-COPY): submitSwapCopy(event, front, back) is a copy into the front, the back WindowServer's again on return; the driver flipped to the back. ECC review round 2 (cpp-reviewer) and security review, all confirmed findings fixed: a lost ready made every later page-off of its surface wait the full bound (the next write's own ready took the forgiven mark, the write's count stayed, the next timeout forgave it again): such a pending mark is a ghost and the next timeout drops it for good, forgiven marks age out after RD3_SP_FORGIVE_MS (1 s), so a ready that never comes costs at most two bounded waits (surface_wait_ghosts, _aged; lost_ready_bounded host test: 8 timeouts in 8 page-offs before, 2 after); any process named WindowServer could hold page-offs on the family lock with its own pending marks: a mark is taken only from a process named WindowServer that runs as _windowserver (uid 88) or is the display client (the pid of its legacy swaps / display-pipe transactions), readies included (rd3_sp_mark_gate, surface_wait_notreal, _ws_pid, _mark_pid, _mark_uid; the boot line and the first mark taken name it), pending marks are refused as MOOT (ENXIO, the plugin's back-off) while no WindowServer surface can be VRAM (rdna3a3ws=0, rdna3a3display=0, no IOP flip; surface_wait_moot), and a timeout budget stops the waits after 4 timeouts with no real ready until one comes (RD3_SPW_SKIP, surface_wait_starved, _skipped, PAGEOFF flag 16); parked VRAM was never given back under pressure: only an allocation a GEM BO was imported over is parked (RD3VidMemory::fBoGen, vm_park_noimport), an allocPhysical refusal releases every park no BO holds any more (the core's rdna3VramRefs count, new) and asks once more, the parked bytes are capped (debug.rdna3accel_vm_park_cap_mb, 512: the oldest parks no BO holds go), counters vm_park_trim_pressure, _trim_cap, _trimmed(_bytes), _trim_held, _trim_unknown, vm_alloc_retry_ok / _fail, VMFREE v2 RD3_TRVMV2_NOBO (the import ends there: no H-VMMOVE); the plugin counts the readies sent at a command buffer's end (its stats line, triage INFO). ECC review round 3 (cpp-reviewer), all confirmed findings fixed: the display client was recorded for any process named WindowServer before the swap / transaction was validated and replaced by the last caller (a renamed process passed the mark gate with one swap; a log line per pid flip): it is recorded only for a legacy swap that names our VRAM or a display-pipe transaction that drives one of our heads, the first one is sticky while it lives (rd3_sp_display_client: replaced only when no process has its pid or it is no longer named WindowServer, proc_name), a second one is refused and counted (surface_wait_ws_pid_refused, triage WARN), both logs rate limited (host test display_client_sticky); deallocPhysical read fPark after dropping gVmParkLock (a trim in between cleared it and vm_parked_bytes went negative): the parked bytes are counted from the copy taken under the lock, before the unlock; the trim looked only at the oldest 128 parks (releasable parks behind 128 a BO holds were never found): it pages through the list (up to 8 pages), dropping the held / unknown ones from each page (rd3_vp_keep_releasable, host test paged_trim); the trim's log line for an unknown core count was not rate limited: first, then every 1024th; between the trim's unlink and its release the object may be re-backed or freed and its address reused, so kVmForgetAll could wipe a newer allocation's records: the trim forgets only the released address (kVmForgetAddr: nothing else holds it before the release), and the records keyed by the object are forgotten by the object itself at its next allocPhysical or free() (RD3VidMemory::fParkTrimmedPhys), while it is alive and before it is backed again. Open: (1) only WindowServer's surfaces are marked pending; by default (rdna3a3ws=0) WindowServer's inputs stay out of A3, so the plugin's marks only engage with rdna3a3ws=1 / rdna3a3display=1 (triage and the boot line say MOOT); an application's own surface captured while its GPU writes it still reads the previous frame (no black: parking keeps the right VRAM); (2) whether IOAccelVidMemory::free calls deallocPhysical is not known (the card's vm_free_live says); (3) parks a BO still holds are never released (releasing them frees nothing; they go with the BO's surface), parks of an older core kext without rdna3VramRefs are never released under pressure (by design: their holders are unknown; counted vm_park_trim_unknown, logged at the first trim and every 1024th with the core's answer, triage WARN kept because the core's reference count was unknown - closed as "log it at least"), and a park the trim releases from another vidmem uses that object's pointer as a key only for its trace (closed, checked 2026-10-23: rd3VmRelease forgets the address before it gives the VRAM back, so no other vidmem can hold that address when its records are forgotten; the object forgets its own records); closed 2026-10-23 (quick/low-items): a trim paged by count, so a park unlinked meanwhile let one be skipped until the next trim - pages now resume after the last listed park's time (rd3_vp_cursor, tests/test_vram_park.c cursor_pages); (4) closed by the forgiven marks and the ghosts; closed 2026-10-23 (quick/low-items): an evicted surface's marks (or a reused forgiven-only slot's) are kept in a ring of 16 (rd3_sp_evict_*): its late readies take them (LATE), a new pending mark carries the rest in as forgiven marks aged from the eviction (surface_wait_evict_kept, triage's eviction WARN, tests/test_surf_pending.c evicted_marks_kept). Still open, needs a decision and the plugin (a per-write token in the marks: ABI change, Metal plugin built on the macOS runner): the table cannot tell which write a ready belongs to, so a ready that takes a forgiven mark while a newer write is pending is ambiguous (the late ready, or the newer write's own): the wait then ends at the next ready (later, never earlier), and a lost ready costs at most two bounded waits, not one; if both the dropped write's late ready and the newer write's ready were lost, the newer write's late ready after the second timeout finds no forgiven mark and can take a third write's count; the timeout budget counts timeouts in a row with no real ready, not per second (bound-independent: a 1 s bound would never trip a per-second count); (5) the ownership check uses IOSurfaceRoot::lookupSurface on the pending-mark path without the family lock, assuming no page-off holds IOSurfaceRoot's lock while it waits (only pending marks look up; readies never block on it); (6) one refused pending mark (e.g. ENOENT for one released surface) switches the plugin to the old order for a second for every surface; (7) when both try-lock newest marks of a surface find the accelerator's lock taken, its ready still goes first (counted: the plugin's stats line, triage WARN). Card must show: vm sticky: 1 and surface wait: 1 at boot; windows no longer grey / stale / flashing; vm_parked close to vm_unparked, vm_dirty_moved and vm_restore_miss 0, H-VMMOVE absent; screenshots / window captures not black; with rdna3a3ws=1 surface_wait_pending = _ready, _timeouts 0; A/B sysctl -w debug.rdna3accel_vm_sticky=0 brings the symptoms back

WindowServer never uses IOPresentment transactions with our driver (N39: page-flip mode, iop_ok 0 on every card run) because CoreDisplay classifies every IOFramebuffer's GPU vendor by its class name. Probe runs 10 and the cfstr peek (macOS 15.8.1 / 24H32, the card VM's build): CoreDisplay::UseIOPresentment() requires every framebuffer to be a known vendor; _GetGPUVendorForFramebufferService (0x7FF80412CC38) takes IOObjectCopyClass(fb) - the LEAF C++ class name, not the service name - and does four case-insensitive CFStringFinds in order: Intel -> 2, AMD -> 4, AppleParavirt -> 0x10, NVDA -> 8, none -> 1 (unknown); ___GetSystemGPUVendors_block_invoke ORs them over all IOFramebuffers and clears bit 1, so RDNA3Framebuffer gave gpuVendors 0 and useIOP was forced off for the session. Every card report shows it: GetSystemGPUVendors_block_invoke: 0x1000002d1 1 RDNA3Framebuffer IOService:/... for both heads (NullMoth's class names contain NVDA). The switches are GetBoolCFPreference lookups in the com.apple.CoreDisplay domain; CoreDisplay's static values: useIOP 1, useIOP_onIG 1, useIOP_onNVIDIA 0, useIOP_onAMD 0, useIOP_onAPV 0 (none set on the runner; whether InitializeUserDefaults changes them at run time is not known - NullMoth works with onNVIDIA statically 0). Done: (1) IOKit instantiates the leaf AMDRDNA3Framebuffer : RDNA3Framebuffer (RDNA3_FB_CLASS in fb/rdna3-fb.cpp, both IOClass strings in fb/Info.plist; fb/Makefile refuses a bundle whose IOClass differs and make check looks for its metaclass; tests/test_fb_class.py keeps source, plist, Makefile and triage equal and the name free of Intel / AppleParavirt / NVDA and of Apple's class names). The service name stays RDNA3Framebuffer (setName), so ioreg paths, serviceMatching("RDNA3Framebuffer") in the accelerator and AGDC and ioreg -c RDNA3Framebuffer in the collectors (a subclass matches) are unchanged. Logged at start: RDNA3-fb: head N class AMDRDNA3Framebuffer, name RDNA3Framebuffer: CoreDisplay classifies the framebuffer by this class name (contains AMD: vendor 4 expected, IOPresentment allowed) (-- NOT AMDRDNA3Framebuffer ... when IOClass is not the leaf). (2) The VM writes useIOP and useIOP_onAMD = true AnyUser/AnyHost (/Library/Preferences/com.apple.CoreDisplay) on every boot, the install boot included (autoreport.sh coredisplay_iop_prefs); WindowServer reads them at its start, so they count from the next boot; rdna3iopprefs=0 in the step's boot-args takes them out (rdna3vm passes it to the install boot too); the tests file says what this boot's WindowServer started with (== CoreDisplay prefs ...), collect.sh packs 19-coredisplay-prefs.txt (tests/test_coredisplay_prefs.sh). Triage present mode, GPU vendor per framebuffer: PASS vendor 4; INFO IOPresentment off: framebuffer classed vendor 1 (unknown) for the old class (old reports); FAIL for the new class still at 1 or another class (another driver's framebuffer; the kext's own -- NOT line is the fb class FAIL); WARN other vendors, no line, no log; present mode, CoreDisplay prefs INFO with useIOP_onAMD at WindowServer's start, WARN when unknown or not written; the planes in transactions FAIL names the vendor-1 classes. The card run must show: GetSystemGPUVendors_block_invoke: ... 4 AMDRDNA3Framebuffer for both heads, CoreDisplay prefs ... started with useIOP_onAMD 1, then debug.rdna3accel_iop_ok > 0 (and init_page_flip no longer on). Open: (a) which user/host the preference lookup uses is not proven: if both heads show 4 and useIOP_onAMD 1 but transactions stay off, write the keys into the _windowserver user's domain too (triage prints this lead as INFO); (b) a new framebuffer binary needs the user's approval in macOS (rdna3vm approve); (c) CoreDisplay may take other AMD-only paths for a vendor-4 framebuffer (watch the CoreDisplay log for AMD-specific errors); (d) kern.boottime was parsed with a greedy .*sec = that captured usec (prune_old_traces in autoreport.sh, collect_plugin_trace.sh, the boot-start record): fixed here (anchored ^{ *sec = , tests/test_prune_traces.sh, tests/test_plugin_trace.sh); an unparseable boot time is said (boot time unknown, prefs record could NOT tell), (e) rdna3iopprefs=0 reaches no install boot when the bundle is current (rdna3vm returns early): keys left by an earlier boot then show as WindowServer started with useIOP_onAMD 1 and triage WARNs this run is NOT the A/B without them (the run after takes them out); the kext's -- NOT AMDRDNA3Framebuffer line is a triage FAIL (present mode, fb class)

Lessons from Navi48-MacOS (https://github.com/Almosst-DEV/Navi48-MacOS, commit d1f7bad, RX 9070 XT on macOS 26.6.2 Tahoe; MIT top level, src/navi48-bringup BSD-3 with its own NOTICE, third-party/metal2vulkan-n48 LGPL-3.0: do not take from it; credit Almosst-DEV in comments and NOTICE for anything ported). All their addresses are from macOS 26, ours is 15.8.1 (24H32): every item is an A/B on the card. Ranked: (1) CoreDisplay's GetBoolCFPreference reads CFPreferencesCopyValue(key, "com.apple.CoreDisplay", kCFPreferencesAnyUser, kCFPreferencesCurrentHost) = the ByHost store (tools/pc/cdpref.c), keys read by _CGXMappedDisplayStart: useIOP, useIOP_onAMD, displaySurfaceUsesIOSurface, useIOP_gamma_onAMD; N44 wrote only AnyHost (/Library/Preferences/com.apple.CoreDisplay); DONE (host tests, card run open): autoreport.sh writes useIOP / useIOP_onAMD also into the ByHost file /Library/Preferences/ByHost/com.apple.CoreDisplay.<IOPlatformUUID> (by path, no -currentHost: as root it would write /var/root/Library/Preferences/ByHost, the wrong store; UUID from ioreg IOPlatformExpertDevice, refused when it holds anything but hex and -), each store reported on its own (AnyHost written; ByHost(<uuid>) could NOT be written (...)), both taken out with rdna3iopprefs=0; collect.sh packs both stores and ls -l ByHost/ into 19-coredisplay-prefs.txt; triage CoreDisplay prefs reports both stores and WARNs when only AnyHost held the keys at WindowServer's start. Only these two keys are written; displaySurfaceUsesIOSurface / useIOP_gamma_onAMD are not (their effect is unknown: the next A/B). Still open: confirm the user/host on 15 with a probe of GetBoolCFPreference (WIP patch docs/handoff/wip-probe-boolpref.patch, unreviewed, not applied). (2) AGDC 0x711 type 0x40 (inline buffer) must be answered: count, MaxWidth, MaxHeight (non-zero), two Float32 latencies, PixelFormats; a zeroed reply gives IOPresentment 0x2004 (capabilities malformed); type 0x10 count 0 gives 0x2006; other types zero-filled + success (src/navi48-bringup/src/apple/display_pipe_guard.h ~352-420). Ours answered only 0x10 and counted types only below 32. (3) A/B: 0x921 link-config flags bit 0 must stay clear (it starts __GatherAGDCLinkCapabilities; ours sets 0x8|0x1|0x2); plane-scaler entry per AMD's getFbCapabilityForScaler: width +0x24, height +0x28, markers 0x00050005 and 4 (ours from NullMoth: height first, 0x70007, 0). (2) and (3) done (host tests; card run open), agdc/rdna3_agdc_n48.h + tests/test_agdc_n48.c, only with K5 on (rdna3agdc=3/4 or debug.rdna3agdc_k5=1), each its own boot-arg + sysctl: rdna3agdcib / debug.rdna3agdc_ib bit mask (bit 0 answer type 0x40: count 1, Flags 1, latencies 1.0f, MaxHeight/MaxWidth = the raster, pixel formats 1; bit 1 zero-fill every capability type 0x1..0x8000 but 0x10 and 0x40 with success; other values and an index past the slot table stay Unsupported as before), default 0 = as before N45 (K5-on boots crash-looped before, runs 41 / 45, and the next card run tests N44 first), 3 = Navi48's set is the A/B arm; rdna3agdclinkflags / debug.rdna3agdc_linkflags 0 = 0xb NullMoth (default), 1 = 0xa, 2 = 0; rdna3agdcscaler / debug.rdna3agdc_scaler 0 = NullMoth (default), 1 = AMD markers 0x50005 / filter 4 with the height first, 2 = Navi48's entry as written (width first). Counters debug.rdna3agdc_ib_answered, _zero_filled, _scaler_alt, _linkflags_last, _types_mask (bit k = type 1 << k), _types (text, per type asked/answered, e.g. 0x10 56/56, 0x40 56/56); log lines RDNA3-agdc: N45 answers (with K5 on): rdna3agdcib=..., K5: 0x711 index N type 0x40 answered: inline buffer MaxWidth W MaxHeight H ..., K5: 0x711 index N type 0x.. zero-filled + success ..., K5: 0x921 index N answered WxH, flags 0x.. ..., K5: 0x711 index N plane scaler answered WxH, <layout> ..., sysctl changes debug.rdna3agdc_ib A -> B: ... (a change counts from the next WindowServer start: IOPresentment gathers capabilities when it builds a display). macOS 26.4.1 (25E253) facts (Tahoe VM, IOPresentment in the dyld cache via dyld_info -disassemble, AMDRadeonX6000Framebuffer from SystemKernelExtensions.kc): __GatherAGDCDisplayPipelineCapabilities bzeroes each 0x196c reply, writes fbIndex and the u64 0x8_000000TT at +4, asks 0x10, 0x8, 0x4, 0x2, 0x1, 0x40, 0x20, 0x200, 0x80, 0x100, 0x400..0x8000; only 0x10 gates (fail or count 0 = 0x2006), any other refused type only leaves its present byte clear, the gather still returns 0; a success count > 8 is cut to 8. IOPresentmentCreateInlineBufferDictionary takes the first entry with +0x0c and +0x10 non-zero: +0 Flags & 0x803fffff, +4/+8 Activation/DeactivationLatency (Float32), +0x0c MaxHeight, +0x10 MaxWidth, +0x14 AGDC pixel-format mask (u32); type 0x40 refused = no InlineBuffer dictionary and __inlineBufferFromDictionary returns 0 without one, so refusing 0x40 is NOT an error on 26 (Navi48's 0x2004 came from their zero-filled 0x40: never zero-filled here). __GatherAGDCLogicalDeviceCapabilities: a failed 0x921 = 0x80e (no capabilities); bit 0 calls __GatherAGDCLinkCapabilities, whose result is ignored: it sets LinkCurrentMaximumBPC to 2 and asks 0x925 / 0x988 (we refuse 0x925), so on 26 bit 0 only changes that key (2 vs 0). __PopulateScalerEntries reads a scaler record (+0x24 / +0x1ac, u64 counts at +0x1c / +0x1a4) as Height, Width, MaxHeight, MaxWidth, pixel formats, filter: entry +0x24 is the HEIGHT (NullMoth's order); Navi48's width-first entry reports the scaler 1080 wide x 1920 high. AMD's AMDRadeonX6000_AmdAgdcServices::getFbCapabilityForScaler writes path+0x720 at +0x24, +0x71c at +0x28, 0x00050005 / 4 at +0x34 / +0x38; AtiDeviceControl::getFbCapabilityForScaler writes 0x70007 (NullMoth's). Lessons not in N45: (1) WindowServer pairs a display with its accelerator at display init through IOAccelFindAccelerator(fb), which reads IOAccelTypes / IOAccelIndex (and IOAccelRevision 2) from the IOFramebuffer (stamped by IOAccelDisplayPipe::init; Navi48 Navi48AccelPeer.cpp ~3210-3264, 26.6.2): ours goes through the family's init (accel/rdna3-accel.cpp newDisplayPipe) but no report shows the keys: add ioreg -l -c AMDRDNA3Framebuffer | grep IOAccel to collect.sh + a triage row before the N44 card run; DONE (host tests, card run open): collect.sh pairing_report packs 16-ioaccel-pairing.txt (per framebuffer instance of RDNA3Framebuffer, which also matches the leaf AMDRDNA3Framebuffer: its IOAccelTypes / IOAccelIndex / IOAccelRevision, RDNA3,Head, RDNA3,Connected, then the registry path of each RDNA3Accelerator built from ioreg -p IOService), triage row present mode, display pairing (pairing_rows, tests/test_triage_pairing.py): PASS per connected head whose IOAccelTypes names RDNA3Accelerator with IOAccelRevision 2 and an IOAccelIndex, WARN for a missing key / another accelerator / another revision / no framebuffer, INFO for an older report or no connected head. The value format of IOAccelTypes on a real driver is not confirmed (the Tahoe VM's paravirtual framebuffer carries none of the keys): the row only looks for the accelerator's class name in the value; (2) Tahoe kext consent (checked on the owner's 26.4.1 VM: spctl kext-consent status ENABLED, csr-active-config 0): every rebuilt kext in /Library/Extensions needs Allow; only IOPCIFamily-only kexts can go into the boot collection from the ESP (Navi48 tools/pc/esp-kext.sh), IOGraphics/IOAccel kexts cannot (System KC only): R4 / N20 packaging; (3) IOPCIDevice's vtable changed on 26 (RX4 docs/m0-pvgpu.md: 11 of 313 slots, configRead/Write 8/16/32 became real virtuals at 272-277): check our kexts' undefined symbols against 26's KCs and add a slot check to tools/check_accel_slots.py; (4) IOGraphicsAccelerator2's orphaned-VRAM pool (MetalCyan, 26.7.1: buffers flagged reusable at creation are never trimmed, GBs stuck): check whether RD3VidMemory hits it (N41); (5) cursor updates are not bracketed by the MPC cursor lock (amdgpu dc_stream.c cursor_lock(true/false) -> mpc1_cursor_lock, CUR_VUPDATE_LOCK_SET; ours hw/dcn32.c cursor_image / cursor_position write without it, and hw/gen has no CUR_VUPDATE_LOCK register): port with a lock model in tests/sim_navi32.c first; (6) Tahoe sandbox profiles (26.4.1, /System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles) allow the family's IOAccel* user-client classes and AMDRadeonX6000_AMDAccelDevice, none of ours: a sandboxed app (Safari's GPU process, N1) cannot open our own classes; (7) packaging hygiene from Navi48/RX4: keep config.prev.plist + plutil -lint, refuse an ESP without OpenCore, check boot-args survive, touch only the EFI OpenCore booted from (boot-path). Card run 2026-10-25, the first on macOS 26.4.1 (Darwin 25.4, step play): only the core RDNA3Accel and RDNA3AGDC loaded; kmutil refused RDNA3Framebuffer and RDNA3Accelerator ("Failed to bind '_thread_call_cancel_wait' ... could not find a kext which exports this symbol"), so the desktop ran without our framebuffer/accelerator. The first fix (com.apple.kpi.* 8.0 -> 19.0 in fb/accel/agdc, as the core) rested on a wrong hypothesis and did not help (card run 2026-10-25b): kmutil libraries -p <kext> --undef-symbols on 26.4.1 shows _thread_call_cancel_wait exported only by com.apple.kpi.private, which a third-party kext in the Auxiliary KC (fb, accel: they depend on IOGraphicsFamily / IOAcceleratorFamily2) cannot link at any version; _kauth_cred_issuser (accel) only by com.apple.kpi.unsupported or private; the core imports both but loads because OpenCore's prelinker does not enforce KPI exports; agdc had none. Done (2026-10-25b): every thread_call_cancel_wait (fb, accel and the core) replaced by common/rdna3_tcall.h (public KPIs: a trampoline counts each run from its arm to its end, the stop cancels and polls with IOSleep and, like thread_call_cancel_wait, never gives up (ECC review round 1: a 30 s give-up was a use-after-free): logged at 2 s, then every 30 s with the call's name, the time and the runs outstanding; a refused enter during a free holds its count until its last access; a failed allocation and a free from inside the call's own run (refused) are logged; RDNA3-tcall: log lines, triage thread call stop from the archive and serial kernel log; host test tests/test_tcall.c); accel and the core declare com.apple.kpi.unsupported 19.0 (accel: an IOP flip whose enter is refused gives its surface back); tests/test_kext_kpi_versions.py fails on any call in a kext source that xnu-12377.101.15 (macOS 26.4) exports only from kpi.private, or only from kpi.unsupported without declaring it (tools/ci/data/, made from xnu's config/.exports by tools/ci/gen_xnu_kpi_lists.py; cross-checked against kmutil on the Tahoe VM); CI's macOS kext job runs tools/ci/kmutil_kpi_guard.py (kmutil libraries + nm -u + the same xnu-12377 lists, since the macOS 15 runner's kmutil cannot see what 26 made private; host test tests/test_kmutil_kpi_guard.py on captured 26.4.1 output) on fb, accel (both layouts) and agdc, failing on a private-only, undeclared or unresolved import (the Tahoe layout's unresolved imports are listed as a warning). Open: (a) the next Tahoe run (rdna3vm update, approve, step play) shows fb and accel linking and starting, then R4 proper (the Tahoe accelerator layout on 26.4); (b) done (2026-10-25, card run open: the core still loads and maps BOs): the core imported IOMemoryDescriptor::doMap / doUnmap through RDNA3BoMemory's vtable (an IOSubMemoryDescriptor subclass that overrides neither), mangled as the public SDK declares them (vm_map_t = ipc_port *: __ZN18IOMemoryDescriptor5doMapEP8ipc_portPyjyy), while xnu-11417 (macOS 15) and xnu-12377 (macOS 26.4) export only their own _vm_map * names (config/IOKit.x86_64.exports), so no kext exported them and only OpenCore's prelinker bound them. xnu never calls either on a sub-range descriptor: createMappingInTask -> makeMapping, which IOSubMemoryDescriptor forwards to the parent; the parent's IOMemoryDescriptor::makeMapping calls doMap on itself (or a withAddressRange copy for kIOMapUnique) and stores that descriptor in the IOMemoryMap, so IOMemoryMap::unmap's fMemory->doUnmap goes to the parent as well (IOSubMemoryDescriptor.cpp, IOMemoryDescriptor.cpp of both tags; IOMemoryDescriptor::doMap itself returns kIOReturnUnsupported). RDNA3BoMemory now overrides both with kIOReturnUnsupported and a log line (RDNA3: GEM: RDNA3BoMemory::doMap called (N such): ..., first 8 then every 1024th; triage BO map FAIL, host test tests/test_triage_bomem.py); the core's imports, checked as object files against every x86_64 config/.exports of both tags, are all exported now, and CI's KPI guard for the core blocks. Found on the way and fixed (2026-10-25, card run open): for the same reason the IOMemoryMap did not hold RDNA3BoMemory either (it held the parent, fVramDev / fDoorbellDev), and IOUserClient::mapClientMemory64 releases the descriptor right after createMappingInTask, so the BO reference was dropped as soon as the map was made: a client could close a VRAM or doorbell BO (GEM_CLOSE or closing the device) with its CPU mapping in place and read and write that VRAM or doorbell after it went to another process (the A3 security fix did not hold). Now, the amdgpu way (drm_gem_mmap_obj's GEM reference held by the VMA): every VRAM / doorbell BO has one RDNA3BoMemory that holds a BO reference for its whole life and maps itself (makeMapping on itself, doMap / doUnmap forwarded to an IOMemoryDescriptor::withAddressRange of exactly the BO's BAR range), so each IOMemoryMap holds the BO until xnu frees it, which unmaps first; the map outlives GEM_CLOSE, clientClose (destroyUserReferences moves the mappings set to the connection port) and the file, and goes with IOConnectUnmapMemory64 or the task's last send right. The BO links to it weakly (rdna3_bo::os_map under fBoMapLock, a leaf lock), so a second map and IOConnectUnmapMemory64's kIOMapReference lookup find the same descriptor; its taggedRelease clears the link before the last reference goes (common/rdna3_bo_map.h, rd3_bomap_release_unlinks; OSObject::taggedTryRetain is xnu-private). The map path also refuses userptr and no-CPU-access BOs (amdgpu_gem_object_mmap; every userptr BO had mmap offset 0, which the old libdrm_xnu's unmap reached). libdrm_xnu unmapped with memory type 0, which named no BO, so every unmap failed and mappings piled up until the device closed: it now passes the BO's offset, and an address mapped twice goes with its last unmap. Counters debug.rdna3_vram bomap_live / bomap_live_kib / bomap_made / bomap_reused / bomap_refused / bomap_nobo / bomap_fail, log lines GEM: CPU map of offset 0x... refused: ... and GEM: CPU map|unmap of a ... BO ... failed in xnu, triage BO map rows and the VRAM heap's INFO line; host tests tests/test_bo_map.c (GEM_CLOSE, file close, shared BO, doorbell, refusals against the real UAPI), tests/test_triage_bomem.py, the loopback backend refuses an unmap whose offset names no mapped BO (tests/test_libdrm_amdgpu.c). ECC review round 1 (security + C++ reviewers; nothing CRITICAL/HIGH introduced), fixed: BOs held only by mappings were bounded by no limit (map + GEM_CLOSE loops, close and reopen): each descriptor is charged to the uid of the file that made it (rdna3_drm_file.owner, rdna3_drm_bomap_charge / _uncharge, dev->bomap per uid), capped at RD3_BOMAP_DEVICE_MAX (65536) descriptors on the device and, for any uid but root / _windowserver, RD_MAX_HANDLES descriptors and twice the visible VRAM (rd3_bomap_quota_admit), and the IOSurface import quota counts the surface VRAM the owner's mappings hold (each BO once, a reopened file still sees it); refusals logged with the reason, bomap_quota; the client's map flags no longer pick the cache mode (doMap forces write-combined for VRAM, uncached for doorbells); withBo's causes are logged with the physical range (no descriptor for the ..., bomap_nodesc); drmFree sets a flag under fBoMapLock and waits for releases in flight, a release after the teardown leaves its BO and logs (went after the DRM teardown); libdrm_xnu keeps a mapping's entry when the unmap fails (a retry no longer reaches libc's munmap) and drops it when the connection goes; tests/test_bo_map_race.c runs the release protocol under 8 threads. ECC review round 2 (security; nothing CRITICAL/HIGH), fixed: the charge was a flag on the BO, so a descriptor made between the previous one's unlink (taggedRelease) and its uncharge (drmBoMapRelease) went uncharged, outside every cap; the charge is now the descriptor's own (rdna3_bomap_charge: generation, size, uid, foreign, exempt; made whenever a descriptor is made, given back exactly), and the BO keeps only the newest charge's generation for file_foreign_bytes' per-BO view; root and _windowserver take no slot of the 64-uid table (they could have been refused TABLE_FULL); each quota reason's first refusal is logged on its own limiter (refused by the quota for the first time); the race test now charges and uncharges under the outer lock and checks the uid's count against the live descriptors. Still open: (b1, HIGH impact per the security review, no cheap complete fix) a task can mach_vm_remap its own mapping (copy = FALSE), share it into a fork with VM_INHERIT_SHARE, or make a memory entry of it (mach_make_memory_entry): each references the device pager's VM object, which populateDevicePager fills for the whole range at map time and xnu keeps populated after the IOMemoryMap is unmapped, so it survives the BO and reaches whatever the range holds next; clearing VRAM on allocation protects only the initial contents, and IOMemoryDescriptor::redirect blocks only pages not yet populated. Research direction (needs a card run): map with kIOMapUnique and take the pages away on the BO's end with IOMemoryMap::redirect (UPL_BLOCK_ACCESS / upl_transpose), as IOFramebuffer does for its VRAM; (b2, LOW) a BO kept alive only by a mapping is in no file's handle table, so rdna3resume's S3 eviction (rdna3_drm_device_for_each_vram_bo) does not save its contents (the range stays owned); (b3, LOW) a kext and a userspace of different builds: an old libdrm_xnu's unmaps still fail, and its freed BOs now stay allocated until the process closes the device (triage BO map WARN for offset 0) - install both from one full: true build; (b4) teardown: done in round 1 (above); what stays is that a BO whose mapping outlives drmFree is leaked, logged; (c) the guard's parser knows 26.4.1's output format only: the first CI run on the macOS 15 runner confirms it reads that one too (an unknown line is an error, not a pass); (d) the xnu lists are of xnu-12377.101.15: regenerate them (gen_xnu_kpi_lists.py --tag <new>) when the VM's macOS moves on ECC round 2 (113d5f8), LOW, open: (e) a drain does not refuse arms, so an HPD storm with rdna3hpdirq=1 and rdna3hotplug=0 keeps displayShutdown / displayMirrorOff / hotplugSuspend waiting until it ends (logged every 30 s): a stop flag checked in displayHotplug, or teardownInterrupt before displayMirrorOff; (f) runner holds only the last thread to enter: self-stop detection is reliable only on ONCE calls (no call site stops from its own run); (g) a refused self-free does not set stopping or cancel what is queued (no call site does it); (h) older than N47: statsStart (from displayWakeWork) and statsStop (displayWillSleep) are not serialized - fWakeCall is not drained before the sleep's statsStop. CI: the Forgejo Mac runner links against Darwin 25 collections (run 42: the Sequoia accelerator's 15-only IOAccelSurface/LegacyDisplay imports unresolved), so the guard step checks the accelerator layout for the runner's own Darwin and only warns for the other. Round 3 note (LOW, open): root and _windowserver skip the per-uid table, so the IOSurface import quota (file_foreign_bytes) no longer counts surfaces they keep CPU-mapped after closing the handle; fine while the Metal plugin does not CPU-map imported surfaces - revisit if it starts to.

Card run 2026-10-25c, Metal plugin on macOS 26.4.1 (Chess, WindowServer, Terminal). (1) Chess (OpenGL over the plugin, apps mode) died deterministically (3 launches) right after newTextureView: depth/stencil view format 260 aspect 6, the texturedFragment2DDepthResolve pipeline and G14 census: copy texture->buffer 971x680. ROOT CAUSE, certain from the three crash reports (Chess-2026-10-25-043835.000.ips / 043835.ips / 043932.ips, copied from the Tahoe disk): EXC_CRASH SIGABRT, __assert_rtn in libvulkan_radeon gfx_or_compute_copy_memory_to_image.cold.1 <- radv_CmdCopyBufferToImage2 <- vk_common_CmdCopyBufferToImage <- RDNA3MTLDriver nvmtl_vk_cmd_copy_buffer_to_image_level_layer +858 <- -[NVMTLBlitCommandEncoder copyFromBuffer:...toTexture:...options:] <- AppleMetalOpenGLRenderer gldBlitFramebufferData <- gldReadFramebufferData <- GLEngine glReadPixels_Exec <- Chess; in our Mesa (d381c361, src/amd/vulkan/meta/radv_meta_copy.c) that function starts with assert(image->vk.samples == 1): Apple's GL renderer uploads a BUFFER into a 4-SAMPLE texture, which Vulkan does not allow (the guess of the first analysis, a texture->buffer copy, was wrong: that census line is only the copy before it; stock RADV 26.2.5 on the dev host does not assert on the image->buffer direction). Fixed in two steps. Step 1 (ff5cb13, host test tests/test_copy_pack.c, metal/plugin/nvmtl_copy_pack.h): both copy functions follow Vulkan valid usage (1 sample, one aspect the format has, depth/stencil element sizes - every depth image said 4 B -, depth/stencil buffer offset a multiple of 4 (the copy is DROPPED is in the log line), non-null VkImage/VkBuffer); a refusal is a log line with the numbers, the G14 census line gains vk format, samples, aspect, element size, Metal pixel format and region, the blit methods refuse objects that are not the plugin's, and the first copy shapes per direction are bracketed by recording / layout transition recorded / vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer recorded lines (a copy that is multisample, depth/stencil or non-colour is always logged, 16 then every 256th). Step 2 (host test tests/test_msupload.c, metal/plugin/nvmtl_msupload.h, probe tests/vk_msupload_probe.c run on the dev host's RADV): the upload into a multisample COLOUR texture is carried instead of refused: a full-screen-triangle pass at the image's sample count (nvmtl_msupload_spv.h, glslc, spirv-val clean; pipelines cached per (view format, samples)) whose fragment shader reads the region's bytes from the buffer by device address (push constants: address, row, origin, size, byte order) and writes them, a covered pixel writes every sample; 8-bit RGBA/BGRA UNORM and sRGB (written through the UNORM twin view, so the bytes are copied, not converted), R8 and RG8; the probe checks 2/4/8 samples, sRGB, an unaligned buffer offset and the pixels around the region byte for byte after a resolve. Still refused with a log line (copy_buffer_to_image: REFUSED - a N-sample image: ...): multisample depth/stencil destinations (they need a depth-export variant of the pass), multisample arrays, other colour formats (RGBA16F, 10-bit ...). The texture->buffer direction of a multisample texture stays refused (Metal's blit cannot read one either) - WRONG: Apple's GL does exactly that for the stencil resolve, carried since N52. Triage (tools/triage_n48.py, test tests/test_triage_n48.py): plugin copy refused, multisample upload, framebuffer fetch, plugin copy bracket. (2) Framebuffer fetch: WindowServer and Terminal build fragment modules with a [[color(0)]] input (translator: input attachment at binding 192+n); the set layout has no such binding and no render pass an input attachment. Done: nvmtl_spirv_fbfetch.h scans the module (host test tests/test_spirv_fbfetch.c, patched module checked with spirv-val), one log line per pipeline (vk: fbfetch: fragment module #N declares ..., the first 8 of that literal, with counters read / declared-only / zeroed), and the experiment NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1 (default off) that replaces each read by a zero vector. IMPLEMENTED in the follow-up (branch quick/fbfetch, host tests tests/test_fbfetch_plan.c / tests/test_spirv_fbfetch.c, probe tests/vk_fbfetch_probe.c, plugin builds on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode, NOT yet seen on the card). Card run 2026-10-25d (artifacts as rectangles over text cells with a red/green (u,v) gradient in almost every app, broken glyphs in Terminal/Maps): the translator's blend/glyph modules read [[color(0)]] (binding 192+n) from an undefined descriptor. Mechanism chosen: render-pass input attachments, because the plugin records with vkCmdBeginRenderPass and the stock RADV 26.2.5 on the dev host (same RX 7800 XT) offers VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering_local_read but NOT VK_EXT_rasterization_order_attachment_access, so the only other route would be rewriting every pass builder to dynamic rendering. Pipeline (nvmtl_fbfetch_prepare, nvmtl_fbf_plan): the fragment module is scanned before the render pass is built; each read of colour n is SERVED when the pipeline has a float colour attachment n (all 1-sample, non-tessellation pipelines), otherwise that read alone is replaced by a zero vector (a module declares colour inputs the pipeline has no attachment for: Terminal 0x7 declared, one attachment; integer formats, multisample targets, tessellation, NVMTL_FBFETCH_ZERO=1 are zeroed, logged and counted per reason). nvk_pipeline.fbread = served mask. Render pass (nvmtl_make_render_pass(p, load, fbin)): colour n is also pInputAttachments[n], both references GENERAL, plus a by-region self-dependency (COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT/WRITE -> FRAGMENT_SHADER/INPUT_ATTACHMENT_READ); the variant key is load bits | fbin << 16 (nvmtl_rp_variant), so a pass without input attachments is byte-for-byte what it was. The subpass-input variables are moved to descriptor set 3 (nvmtl_spirv_fbfetch_set, before the 0 -> 1 retarget; the pipeline layout of such pipelines has 4 sets, an empty layout in the heap's slot when the device has no heap), an INPUT_ATTACHMENT set of its own (bindings 192..199, fragment): set 1's dirty tracking/reuse/copy logic is untouched. Per pass, at the first draw that reads (nvmtl_fbf_prepare_pass): a 2D view per input attachment (level/slice/format of the attachment, usage INPUT_ATTACHMENT) retired with the command buffer, one set from a dedicated pool (freed with the other sets), written once; per draw (nvmtl_fbf_bind, from nvmtl_vk_cmd_bind_set): pipeline barrier (same masks, by region) + bind set 3. Colour render targets now get VK_IMAGE_USAGE_INPUT_ATTACHMENT_BIT next to COLOR_ATTACHMENT (not 3D volumes; the placement size query uses the same bits). Encoder (-nvmtlFetchPass: in NVMTLObjects.m, called for the pipeline actually drawn, sampler variants included): a pass begins with the first pipeline's inputs; a later pipeline that reads an attachment the pass lacks ENDS the pass and BEGINS it again with LOAD and the union of the inputs (the mesh stage's restart; the visibility query is closed and reopened), counted and logged (vk: fbfetch: ... RESTARTED). Never a silent undefined read: a pass whose attachment cannot be an input (layered pass, 3D slice, no usage, view failure, descriptor-set allocation failure, a pass that lacks the input) SKIPS the draw (c->nobind) with a counted log line. Probe result on the dev host's RADV (stock Mesa 26.2.5; the plugin's Mesa d381c361 was not fetched/compared): 1920x1080 (DCC on) B8G8R8A8 UNORM/SRGB (sRGB view; UNORM view over an sRGB image), R8G8B8A8, RGBA16F, RGBA32F: gradient then a fetch draw, 0 wrong of 8.3 M values; 40 overlapping fetch draws that add 0.01 each in RGBA32F: exact (0 wrong pixels), and 333959 wrong pixels with the barrier removed (NOBARRIER=1 control, so the test does bite); restart shape (pass A without inputs, pass B LOAD with inputs, same framebuffer): exact. Still UNSUPPORTED (zero vector + counted log line, UNSUPPORTED (reason)): multisample colour targets (needs an MS image type + Sample operand in the module), integer colour formats, layered passes, slices of 3D textures, tessellation pipelines, imageblock/tile-shader inputs (bindings 200..479 are not touched by this work). Review round 1 (ECC: cpp-reviewer approved, silent-failure-hunter 3 HIGH, fixed): nvlog keeps the first 8 calls per FORMAT LITERAL, so every refusal reason and every skip cause has a literal of its own, zeroing/scan/move failures are logged unconditionally, a SUMMARY line (nvlog_uncapped, key=value, <= every 10 s, powers of two of error counters, at exit) carries all totals and triage reads the newest (nvmtl_log_cap.h, host test tests/test_fbfetch_plan.c simulates the cap); a skipped fetch draw is counted (c->skipped) and traced NOT DRAWN; the severest reason is reported; a restart keeps LOAD for good. Open / watch: (a) render-pass compatibility: a pipeline's own pass (p->rp) and the begun variant can differ in their input-attachment lists (strictly the spec wants compatible passes; benign on RADV 26.2.5, WATCH on d381c361); (b) write-after-read: an ordinary draw after a fetch draw on the same pixels has no barrier of its own; the probe's interleaved case (60 fetch/ordinary draws, RGBA32F) is exact on 26.2.5. Risks: it is unverified on the card and on the plugin's own Mesa build (GENERAL colour layout + self-dependency + a barrier per fetching draw cost performance; a stale rpx ceiling of 1020 variants per pipeline is now shared with the input-attachment variants); A8 render targets are read through an identity-swizzle R8 view (alpha-only fetch reads R). (3) Per-draw encode timing: trace record kind 17 (NVT_K_ENC_SPLIT) per render encoder that drew: pipeline lookup / descriptor writes / surface-in checks in us, draws, variants built on the draw, the slowest draw (cost with the trace off: a load and a branch per site); tools/plugin_trace.py prints it (encoder cb N: D draw(s) in ..., plus an encode-time split summary block; test in tests/test_plugin_trace.sh). Card runs 2026-10-25c..e (macOS 26.4.1): Activity Monitor shows GPU 0 % for every process, while the accelerator's PerformanceStatistics said Device Utilization % = 100 at idle. What Activity Monitor reads (read on the owner's 26.4.1 VM from the binaries, certain): GPU History = SafeEjectGPU.framework's -[SafeEjectGPUSession getPercentUtilization], PerformanceStatistics / Device Utilization % of the GPU's IOAccelerator; the per-process % GPU / GPU Time = SafeEjectGPU's apps_under_service, which walks the accelerator's subtree and, for entries that conform to IOAccelSubmitter2 (IOAccelContext2 and its 2D/GL/CL/video contexts, IOAccelCommandQueue; IOAccelDevice2 only names the API, IOGPUDeviceUserClient's AppUsage is the IOGPUFamily form), parses IOUserClientCreator (pid %lu, %s) and sums accumulatedGPUTime per pid, the newest lastSubmittedTime marking it active. The family creates both numbers in IOAccelSubmitter2::start and adds to them only from its block fences (IOAccelBlockFence::notifyClient), which our jobs (DRM bridge) never use. The kernel's per-task GPU time (task_power_info_v2.gpu_energy.task_gpu_utilisation, ml_gpu_stat_update) is fed only through gpu_accumulate_time (osfmk/kern/energy_perf.c), exported by com.apple.kpi.private alone in xnu-12377.101.15 (ml_gpu_stat_update by no KPI): Apple's Intel GPU kexts call it, AMD's and IOAcceleratorFamily2 do not; kmutil refuses it (N47), so not used. The SMU value: amdgpu's gpu_busy_percent is METRICS_AVERAGE_GFXACTIVITY (smu_v13_0_0_get_smu_metrics_data -> AverageGfxActivity, the table SMU 13.0.7 shares), which hw/smu13.c reads already; it is 100 % at idle on this card (P2), so it cannot be fixed by porting. Done: common/rdna3_gpu_busy.h (tests/test_gpu_busy.c): the kernel rings' busy time and the GPU time per process in amdgpu's fdinfo model (amdgpu_ctx_fence_time: finished - scheduled; scheduled = our submission, finished = amdgpu_fence_process's fence read from the EOP interrupt, a waiter or the take; a job queued behind another starts at the previous one's end, so a ring's per-job times add up to its busy time); gfx11_fence_read / sdma60_fence_read (amdgpu_fence_read, sim-checked); the core takes it under a spinlock in RDNA3Bringup::submit / fenceSignaled / handleInterrupt and answers RD3K_GPUBUSY_FN (rdna3GpuBusyTake); the accelerator's 250 ms perfstats tick publishes Device Utilization % / GPU Activity(%) (any ring with work) and Renderer / Tiler Utilization % (the GFX ring) over the last second (common/rdna3_gpu_util.h), and adds each process's GPU time to the accumulatedGPUTime of its first IOAccelSubmitter2 under the accelerator (OSNumber::addValue, as the family does; lastSubmittedTime too); a core that does not answer: the SMU value as before, counted. (6) The quit needs no consent: SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGKILL with a wait after each, quit: pid N ended by SIGINT after Ns per instance, triage row ladder quit (a signal skips the plugin's exit SUMMARY; its 10 s lines are the evidence). (7) nvlog lines are cut at 1023 characters now (767 before: the long SUMMARY lines were truncated). About the early fences of 25f (WindowServer 5 in 91994 witnessed submits, worst 867 us, Chess once) no rung switches the kernel side: sync (NVMTL_ASYNC_COMMIT=0) of ladder is the nearest plugin-side check, and fence early per process is the number to watch; a fence that completes before the GPU's writes are visible would hit a CPU readback right after it (a page-off copy), a kext/hw question left to the manager. Surface-in cost (card run 2026-10-25e, WindowServer: surface-in checks 38 % of the encode time, 104 us per call, windows get slower with more elements). MEASURED from the report (tools/plugin_trace.py data joined by command buffer) and on the Tahoe VM (IOSurfaceGetSeed 0.003 us, the VRAM tag query 0.8 us, the pending-write scan 1.3 us, the surface monitor 0.06 us, an upload's lock/unlock 1.9 us): of the 1607 ms, 466 surface UPLOADs are 399 ms (25 %, 855 us each: window surfaces of 971x680 that their producer rewrites per frame, seed moved), the cheap seed checks about 12 ms (1.6 to 5.5 us per call), and ~1100 ms (75 %) are calls of 30 to 300 us in command buffers WITHOUT any SURF_IN record: buffer textures the GPU cannot alias (buffer texture: ... copy (pitch), 112 of the first 128, bytesPerRow not a multiple of 256) are private images refreshed from the buffer's CPU bytes once per command buffer, each refresh a synchronous submit + wait (nvmtl_vk_image_write, ~80 us even for a few KB) that the trace never recorded (the earlier reading 'a lock, a seed query and a tag check per call' was wrong: the decision is not the cost). The same path is the image churn: copy textures were never parked (_bparkable = !copied), so each new one made and later destroyed an image. DONE (plugin, host test tests/test_surfin_cache.c, metal/plugin/nvmtl_surfin_cache.h, built on macOS 26.4.1 Xcode, NOT seen on the card; kill switch NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=1, also in the PARANOID profile (15 switches), logged vk: surfin cache ON/OFF and in vk: ab switches): (1) a lock-free probe answers a surface texture whose seed (and tag generation for a private image) did not move before the pending-write scan and the monitor (the decision is the same as the locked path's first test, which still decides every miss); (2) a buffer texture's refresh compares its source with a shadow of the bytes it last uploaded and skips an equal one (shadow = the exact bytes the upload reads, valid only after a successful upload, dropped by nvmtl_surf_dirty (every blit / mipmap / resolve write) and both replaceRegion variants; up to 1 MiB per image, 32 MiB per process, padding bytes ignored); (3) a pool of 16 private copy images (4 MiB) parked by shape at dealloc and reused by the next copy texture (the new texture's first refresh always uploads); (4) vk: surfin: SUMMARY (nvlog_uncapped, <= every 10 s, at exit: calls, fast, misses by reason, locked uploads / skips, refreshes uploaded / skipped / without shadow / failed / forgotten with their us, shadow bytes, copy images new / reused / parked / evicted), triage row surface-in cache, and ENC_SPLIT words 0..7 (buffer-texture refresh uploads / skips with their us, probe hits, monitor wait, surface uploads; tools/plugin_trace.py). Expected: the ~1100 ms mostly gone for textures whose bytes do not change between frames (an upload of a few KB is ~80 us, an unchanged compare 1 to 3 us), the probe saves 1 to 2 us on each of the 15 K calls; the 399 ms of real surface uploads is NOT cut. Open: (a) the card run (compare bufcopy_skipped / bufcopy_uploaded and the ENC_SPLIT extras with 2026-10-25e; NVMTL_NO_SURFIN_CACHE=1 first if an artefact appears); (b) the surface uploads themselves (no dirty range exists, only the seed; a producer's per-frame copy-out is a full upload): record small uploads into the command buffer instead of a synchronous submit + wait, or share the producer's VRAM (the 89 refused kernel imports, EPERM, are why those surfaces are private); (c) no ladder rung for the switch (the PARANOID rung covers it); (d) the per-draw cost outside surface-in (60 % of the encoder time is the app's own, not split) ECC round 1 (cpp-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter) fixed: replaceRegion forgets the shadow AFTER the image write (both variants; host test), a refresh is skipped only when the image rests in SHADER_READ_ONLY (bufcopy_skip_refused_layout), the SUMMARY comes from a 10 s timer with seq / t_ms (WindowServer never exits cleanly) and carries sin_us_total, fast_us, the refresh times, the calls breakdown, noshadow us, forgotten, refusals by cause, cimg_on, NVMTL_SURFIN_VERIFY=N (every Nth skip reads the image back and compares: the hard stale-image number, FAIL in triage), triage prints all of it and WARNs for a WindowServer without a SUMMARY. Chess artefacts in EVERY rung of the 2026-10-25f A/B ladder (cyan streaks on the pieces, small red bits; PARANOID included, so not scheduling or reuse). ROOT CAUSE, certain from the plugin logs of all 8 Chess processes (the 7 ladder rungs and the main log; reports/2026-10-25f, 26-log-metal-plugin.txt and 41-ladder-*.log, 8 to 16 times per ~70 s per process): Apple's GL resolves the STENCIL of its 4-sample D32_S8 framebuffer (glBlitFramebuffer; Metal's blit has no stencil resolve, the depth goes through the texturedFragment2DDepthResolve draw) in five steps, all visible in the log: (1) blit 4-sample stencil -> buffer (copy_image_to_buffer, vk format 130, aspect option 4 = stencil, bytesPerRow 3884 = 971 * 4 samples * 1 B, buffer offset 0, a 4 MiB buffer); (2) blit the buffer -> a NEW 4-sample R8_UINT texture (image_create: 971x680 4-sample colour target, copy_buffer_to_image, vk format 13, the same row 3884); (3) a render pass with a RESOLVE attachment 4-sample R8_UINT -> 1-sample R8_UINT (begin_render: par1 4-sample pass resolves colour 0x0 of 0x1; an integer resolve takes sample 0); (4) blit the 1-sample R8_UINT -> buffer (row 971, copy_image_to_buffer: recording vk format 13); (5) blit the buffer -> the 1-sample D32_S8 stencil (copy_buffer_to_image: recording (depth/stencil, ...) vk format 130 ... aspect 4 element 1 B). The plugin DROPPED (1) (REFUSED - the image is multisampled, kept refused by N48 on the guess that Metal's blit cannot read a multisample texture - wrong: Apple's GL does it for the stencil) and (2) (REFUSED - a 4-sample image: this colour format is not carried, R8_UINT was outside the N48 upload pass), so (3)-(5) copied an UNINITIALISED 4-sample texture into the 1-sample stencil once per resolve; the board reflections are stencil-masked, hence garbage streaks that no scheduling switch can change. What Apple's blit does with a multisample texture (found from the numbers, the only evidence there is): the buffer row is width * samples * bytes (3884), i.e. element x * samples + s is sample s of pixel x; nothing in the log says more, and the two copies of the chain only have to agree with each other. Done (plugin metal/plugin, host tests tests/test_msdl.c + tests/test_msupload.c, probes tests/vk_msdl_probe.c + tests/vk_msupload_probe.c, triage rows in tools/triage_n48.py): (a) download pass, nvmtl_msdl.h (decision, pure C99) + nvmtl_msbuf.h (the layout and the word walk) + nvmtl_msdl_spv.h (glslc, spirv-val clean): copy_image_to_buffer of a 2/4/8-sample 2D texture runs a compute shader (nvmtl_vk_cmd_copy_msimage_to_buffer) that reads the image through a SAMPLED view of the aspect (texelFetch per sample: stencil through a usampler2DMS view of the stencil aspect, depth through texture2DMS, colour through the UNORM twin of an sRGB image) and writes the buffer by device address, one invocation per aligned 32-bit word (a word that is only partly inside the region is merged with two atomics, so the bytes around the region, an unaligned offset and the row padding are never touched); carried: stencil of S8/D16_S8/D24_S8/D32_S8, depth of D16/D32/D16_S8/D32_S8 (24-bit depth refused, no byte layout in Metal), colour RGBA8/BGRA8/R8/RG8 UNORM and sRGB, R8/RG8/RGBA8/R16/R32 UINT; every 4-sample D/S image already has SAMPLED usage (NVMTL_DEPTH_USAGE), colour MS images too, so no image-creation change was needed (a missing usage is refused with a line); (b) upload pass extended (nvmtl_msupload.h): R8/RG8/RGBA8/R16/R32 UINT through a second fragment module with an unsigned output (R8_UINT is Chess's), and Apple's layout: a row of at least width * samples * bytes is sample-interleaved, one draw per sample restricted by the shader's SampleMask (a shorter row keeps the old one-element-per-pixel semantics); (c) logs and counters (no sysctl: per-process rate-limited lines with running counts, like msupload:): msdl: N-sample image WxH -> buffer (vk format F, aspect A via view V, mode M): region ... row R B, K word(s) in GXxGY group(s) [S so far] (first 8, then every 1024th), msdl: pass built for the ... module, msdl: descriptor pool N opened, msdl: FAILED - ... [F failed, R refused, D done so far], msdl: REFUSED - ..., copy_image_to_buffer: REFUSED - a N-sample image: <reason> (...), the G14 census line now says multisample, carried by the plugin's download pass (view format V, mode M), and msupload: lines name the layout (sample-interleaved / one element per pixel) and the output (integer / float); triage rows multisample download (INFO when it ran, WARN for msdl: FAILED) and plugin copy refused (also for msdl: REFUSED), test tests/test_triage_n48.py; docs/AFTER-TESTS.html has the lines and the Chess stencil-resolve paragraph. Probe on the dev host's RX 7800 XT (RADV Mesa 26.2.5; the plugin's Mesa d381c361 was not compared): tests/vk_msdl_probe.c fills 2/4/8-sample D32_S8 and D16 images with a different stencil and depth value per (10x10 tile, sample) (draws with a static sample mask, depth from the viewport range, stencil by REPLACE with a dynamic reference), downloads whole images and sub-regions at unaligned offsets with padded rows and 0xAA guard bytes: every byte equal, nothing outside touched (320 draws, 971x680 whole image in 0.6 ms); colour roundtrip upload -> download for RGBA8, BGRA8, both sRGB, R8, RG8, R8_UINT, RG8_UINT, RGBA8_UINT, R16_UINT, R32_UINT at 2/4/8 samples with unaligned source/destination offsets: every sample equal; and Chess's whole chain at 971x680 (stencil -> buffer row 3884 -> 4-sample R8_UINT -> vkCmdResolveImage -> 1-sample R8_UINT -> buffer row 971): all 660280 pixels equal sample 0 of the stencil. Negative control (expected samples permuted) fails 178500 bytes, so the probe bites. tests/vk_msupload_probe.c (push constants and the integer module changed) 13 cases 0 wrong. The plugin builds on the macOS 26.4.1 VM (Xcode), 333 warnings (unchanged, none in this code). Other Chess findings (same logs): nothing else wrong - reflection: ... has no AIR, no AIR for the GL helper shaders and the fixIndexBuffer / dsTextureToBuffer compute kernels (SPIR-V cache HITs) are normal; dsTextureToBuffer reads stencil views (newTextureView: depth/stencil view format 261 aspect 4) right after step 5 (inferred: of the 1-sample stencil), so it too depends on this fix; the framebuffer fetch never fires in Chess (no vk: fbfetch: line), msupload had never run on the card before. Open / risks: (1) the order of elements inside a pixel's samples is inferred (sample index fast); it only matters if Apple reads the buffer with the CPU or another shader between the copies, and the log would show that as a copy_image_to_buffer: ... row for the same buffer (it does not in Chess); an integer resolve is sample 0, so the result is the stencil's sample 0 whatever the order; (2) Apple's blit may have meant a resolve (average) for colour/depth - the plugin's copy keeps every sample, which is the only reading that fits a buffer of samples elements per pixel; (3) a compute pass per resolve costs ~0.2 ms for 971x680 on the dev host, cheap; the first use per process builds two pipelines (logged); (4) D24_S8 depth, multisample arrays, RGBA16F/10-bit colour and a multisample depth/stencil UPLOAD stay refused with a line; (5) the sampled view of a depth/stencil image leaves it in SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL (the existing copy/render paths transition from the tracked layout, as after any sampled use). (F3) the flat-layout fallback is counted (flat, up_flat_F), has its own msupload: FLAT layout fallback (why) line and a WARN row; (F4) a literal per refusal / failure kind, the distinct reason of a plan refusal logged at its 1st, 2nd, 4th ... occurrence, per-kind totals in the SUMMARY; (F5) the census says format/aspect carried (the region is decided at the copy); (F6/F7) triage strips the counters from reasons, keeps the parentheses inside them, shows 8, and takes totals from the SUMMARY; (F8) no-device and no-address are separate kinds, the pool-full message carries pools and sets, no meaningless (0) codes, blit copyFrom...: FAILED lines carry the reason (nvmtl_vk_copy_reason); (cpp 1) the download puts the source image back into the layout it had (a following LOAD pass trusts the tracked layout);
