Kernel GPU-memory coherence after card run 2026-10-26b (every plugin switch of ladder-glyphs, PARANOID included, leaves Terminal's incrementally drawn text and Chess's MSAA pieces broken) and the plugin's "fence early" witness (2026-10-25f: WindowServer 5 of 91994 submits, worst 867 us; Chess 1, 19 us; 2026-10-26b: 0 of 51796). AUDIT against amdgpu 6.12 (default profile, rdna3amdgpu=0; certain = from code): (a) gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_fence / user fence, gfx_v11_0_emit_mem_sync (ACQUIRE_MEM GCR GL2/GLM WB+INV, GL1/GLV/GLK/GLI INV), gfx_v11_0_ring_emit_ib_gfx/compute, emit_hdp_flush (CP0<<pipe from the PFP, CP2<<pipe on MEC), emit_vm_flush (+PFP_SYNC_ME), emit_cntxcntl: identical packets (certain); ours emits the mem sync at every job where amdgpu needs AMDGPU_IB_FLAG_EMIT_MEM_SYNC (stronger). (b) amdgpu_vm_flush's pipeline sync before a VM flush and amdgpu_ib_schedule's pipeline sync for an explicit / same-ring implicit dependency (amdgpu_cs_sync_rings -> explicit_sync): the default path never emits it (certain); the CS ioctl waits for SYNCOBJ_IN / DEPENDENCIES on the CPU instead (covers explicit deps), and VMIDs are per file (no VMID reuse under a running job), but there is NO implicit sync for a surface two processes share: a reader's job may overlap the writer's on the ring unless userspace waited for the writer's fence (inferred: matters only without synchronous commits) -> switch pipe_sync. (c) The fence's place: amdgpu keeps it in adev->wb (GTT, system memory; amdgpu_fence_driver_start_ring), ours in a VRAM wb slot read through the BAR (certain). The witness's data are in GTT (memory type 2): with the fence in VRAM the CPU can see the fence (a local VRAM write, read by a BAR read) before the job's GL2 write-back to system memory (posted PCIe writes) is visible; in amdgpu both go the same path (inferred mechanism of "fence early", rare and short as observed). (d) sdma_v6_0_ring_emit_fence (FENCE MTYPE UC + TRAP), emit_hdp_flush, emit_mem_sync (GCR_REQ): same packets; the default DMA path has no in-ring HDP / VM flush (the commit's MMIO HDP flush and vmMap's MMIO TLB flush of both hubs cover them; certain). (e) PTEs: gmc_v11_0_get_vm_pte's MTYPE_UC override for COHERENT / UNCACHED / EXT_COHERENT BOs was missing (certain; RADV also asks UC in the VA flags for those, so no change in practice); gmc_v11_0_map_mtype same; system pages are always SNOOPED (amdgpu: only cached TT; ours is the stronger, coherent choice); every user BO NC by default as amdgpu. Two VMIDs map an IOSurface's VRAM both NC (GL2 is physically tagged, the next job's GL2_INV covers CPU writes; no MTYPE conflict found). (f) HDP 6.0 has no read cache (hdp_v6_0_funcs has no invalidate_hdp, no HDP_READ_CACHE_INVALIDATE register): nothing to invalidate around CPU BAR reads (certain). (g) BUG (certain, fixed): hw/xfer.c (the IOSurface page-off and evict / restore through the 16 MiB GART window) flushed only the MMHUB TLB, but SDMA 6.0 translates through the GFXHUB (sdma_v6_0_sw_init: ring->vm_hub = AMDGPU_GFXHUB(0); amdgpu_gart_invalidate_tlb flushes every hub): the second chunk of a copy larger than the window, or a copy after another one with other pages, went to / came from the previous pages; the staged page-off of card run 26b reuses one contiguous staging buffer, which hid it there (a new staging buffer, a per-page page-off or an evict after it would not be). (h) Freed BOs: amdgpu keeps a BO's memory until its fences signal (TTM delayed delete) and clears unmapped PTEs only after the VM's jobs (amdgpu_vm_clear_freed syncs on the root resv); ours frees and unmaps at once (certain; harmless while userspace waits, as the plugin's PARANOID profile does; open). DONE: hw/gmc11.c gmc11_gart_tlb_flush (both hubs) used by xfer() (sim: the GFXHUB VMID0 TLB is modelled for SDMA GART accesses, S.sdma_tlb_stale; the old code fails the new checks, a second window of other pages and the way back); rdna3_gfx_ring / rdna3_sdma_ring fence_sys_mc / fence_sys_cpu: every fence of the GFX, compute and SDMA rings is ALSO written to a system page bound in the GART under the user-queue slots (RDNA3Bringup::cohSetup at the end of hwInit, again after a reset / wake; self-test per ring, a copy that does not land is switched off and logged), the same RELEASE_MEM / FENCE packet, before the VRAM one, no interrupt (sim asserts both packets); each fence check reads the VRAM value then the copy (common/rdna3_coh.h rd3_coh_fence_check, tests/test_coh.c) and counts a VRAM value ahead of its copy (skews, their length, max): the measured form of "fence early"; gmc_v11_0_get_vm_pte's UC override ported (cohPte, counted uc_bo). IOGraphics' own order is gate -> accelerator lock (its framebuffer notifications reach the display machine through IOAccelDisplayPipe::display_change_handler inside the gate; probeAccelerator too); our thread call took them the other way round. Timing decides whether AppleGPUWrangler's message to the newly published head 1 overlaps the registration, hence -cpu host vs Skylake. FIX (done): RDNA3Accelerator::runInFbGates closes the controller gate of every published head (IOFramebuffer::getWorkLoop, the IOGraphicsControllerWorkLoop; recursive for its owner; each workloop once, head order, common/rdna3_fbgate.h, tests/test_fbgate.c with a lock-order model) through nested IOWorkLoop::runAction before registerFramebufferLocked / deliverModeChange (sysctl debug.rdna3accelfb and the boot-arg thread call); lock order fRegLock -> controller gates -> accelerator lock. ECC review of 0535fd7: a head can publish while the thread waits for a gate (fbPublished sets fFBs[] without fRegLock), so inside the innermost gate the heads are planned again and the body runs only when every gate they need is held (else all gates opened and planned again, at most RD3_FBGATE_TRIES 4, then NOTHING registered); registerPendingLocked hands only heads of that plan to found_framebuffer (a later one: published after its controller gate was planned ... NOT registered now, left to its own boot-arg thread call, queued after fFBs[] is set, or the next sysctl write); the family's sweep (debug.rdna3accelfb=2) is refused while a published head is outside the gates. EVIDENCE (certain from the report and the code): all 0 legacy swaps / copy forwards; every frame reaches the panel through RD3DisplayPipe::performTransaction's CPU COPY of the presented IOSurface's system memory (iop: K6 transaction ... from the IOSurface's memory, debug.rdna3accel_iop_flip 0); WindowServer's display surfaces are kept out of A3 (a3display_refused 99), so the Metal plugin draws them into a private image and puts the pixels into the IOSurface with the CPU only after the GPU finished (-nvmtlSurfaceOut, census: ... 27780 copied out); the plugin trace shows WindowServer composites each display (surfaces 1 / 10, load=Load) and then fills one buffer of a small present ring per frame (surfaces 24 / 34 / 36 in turn). Nothing orders the copy-out before the transaction's copy: a transaction that arrives first copies the frame the buffer held a ring ago = the owner's step back. Whether WindowServer's present path waits for completion (commitAndWaitUntilSubmitted waits, NullMoth's rule) or only for SCHEDULED (SkyLight / QuartzCore also use waitUntilScheduled / addScheduledHandler:, and the plugin marks a command buffer scheduled at its first submit, before any IOSurface publish) is NOT certain from the code: the card run decides. MEASUREMENT + FIX: (1) kernel, common/rdna3_iop_stale.h (tests/test_iop_stale.c): every IOP copy compares the buffer's IOSurface seeds (IOSurface::getLockSeed / getUnlockSeed, moved by the copy-out's write lock) with those at this head's last copy of it; unchanged = STALE (nothing wrote it since), then the copy waits, bounded, polling every 1 ms (debug.rdna3accel_iop_stale_wait_ms, boot-arg rdna3iopstalewait, default 12, 0 = count only; only the done write ends a wait (the unlock seed moved; a lock seed alone is a writer still copying); the wait is bounded by elapsed time; timed-out waits are capped at 48 ms per head per one-second window (iop_stale_capped counts the windows, logged), a wait that saw the write is not charged (ECC review of 5a8765b)) - the port of amdgpu's implicit sync of a scanout BO (amdgpu_dm_commit_planes -> dma_resv_wait_timeout); counters debug.rdna3accel_iop_stale{,_checked,_fresh,_moved,_timeout,_nowait,_capped,_torn,_waitus,_wait_maxus}, log iop stale: ... (rate-limited, first 8 then every 1024th). iop_stale_moved > 0 PROVES the race (the write came during the wait). (2) plugin, metal/plugin/nvmtl_handoff.h (tests/test_handoff.c): per command buffer that writes an IOSurface, whether its scheduled point was observed (a scheduled handler dispatched / waitUntilScheduled returned / -status read Scheduled; the gap is measured from the moment it was marked scheduled) before its last IOSurface write was published, vk: handoff: SUMMARY cbs= early= by_handler= by_wait= by_status= gap_us_avg= gap_us_max= held= held_us_avg= hold= and handoff (N59): command buffer N wrote IOSurface S; its scheduled point was seen ... CERTAIN FROM CODE (CoreImage 26.4.1 decompiled with REA/Ghidra): CoreImage turns the CIKL source into Metal source of [[stitchable]] functions (CIKLLibraryMaker, -[CIKernelLibrary initWithSource:error:] = newLibraryWithSource, language 4.0), then CIKernelReflection::reflect(CIKernelLibrary*, name) reads the library's MTLFunction: functionType (5 = stitchable), returnType.dataType, and arguments (each type, dataTypeDescription.dataType, struct typeName / members) into a vector of argument types; a function with no arguments is accepted. consolidate() copies the FOSL (source) reflection's argument types over that vector element by element with no size check: the library's vector was empty (-[NVMTLFunction arguments] answered nil for any visible function that is not an !air.ci function or a air.stitching_info stitch result), the source's was not, so the first store went through NULL. +[...CIKernelLibrary refelectionWithFunctionName:] is not involved (our library does not answer reflectionForFunctionWithName:, CoreImage reads the function). FIX (plugin, nvmtl_ci_resolved, NVMTLObjects.m; pure part nvmtl_ci_refl.h, tests/test_ci_refl.c): arguments / returnType of a visible function come from the first source that finds an argument: !air.ci, the stitching entry, and NEW a plain !air.visible entry (ptr @f, !{outputs}, !{inputs}, inputs by air.arg_type_name / air.arg_name, the 14 CoreImage struct names of reflect() as one argument each); the function-node match now accepts a quoted name (ptr @"ns::f",, LLVM quotes names with ::), which the old bare match skipped. When none finds an argument the answer is ONE poison argument (binding type 18, data type None): reflect() refuses it (CIKernelError 3, Unsupported type for parameter), _initWithString: then skips consolidate() and builds the kernel from the FOSL reflection alone (the path of CI_USE_MTL_DAG_FOR_CIKL_SRC=0) instead of crashing; the plugin logs CI REFL FAILED <fn>: no argument list could be read ... and up to 36 CI REFL FAILED diag <fn>: <AIR line> lines (the define, the !air.visible / !air.ci lists and the nodes naming the function), so the AIR shape is in the report if it still comes to that. (2) the GPU copy-out (default ON, NVMTL_SURFOUT_DMA=0 = the old path, also in the NVMTL_PARANOID profile and vk: ab switches): at a part's seal, before it can be submitted, each private surface image it wrote is copied into the IOSurface's own pages at the end of its command buffer (nvmtl_surface_dma_record -> nvmtl_vk_cmd_surface_dma: vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer at the surface's bytes per row and plane offset, then a transfer -> host barrier); the pages are imported once per texture (nvmtl_so_import, at the publish of its first copy-out, which is a CPU one, on the execution thread, so per-frame wrappers never import and the commit thread never wires pages) through nvmtl_vk_buffer_import_host (GEM_USERPTR, system memory the GPU maps snooped, gpuvm_pte_flags), only when nvmtl_so_plan accepts the geometry (single sample, no blocks, 1/2/4/8/16-byte elements, page-aligned base, plane offset and pitch whole elements, rows inside the allocation) and every page is writable in the process (mach_vm_region: a read-only page would be wired read-only and a GPU write there a VM fault). The publish (-nvmtlSurfaceOutDMA) only takes and gives back the IOSurface write lock so the seeds move (the kernel's N59 gate, surface-in, every other reader see the new frame); N59's hold then ends at GPU completion. A surface whose base moved since the import gets the CPU copy-out and no GPU copies again (why_moved); every other reason keeps the CPU path for that texture and is counted (why_off/first/shape/ro/import/moved/record). Frame trace path 7 COPY-OUT(GPU into the surface, N61). (3) kernel: the IOP copy moves 8 bytes per access when every address and length is 8-aligned (same bytes, half the bus transactions). NOT DONE: the IOP copy by SDMA (A2's linear copies, hw/xfer.c): it needs the IOSurface's pages GART-mapped in the kernel per transaction and a wait for the SDMA fence before performTransaction returns (the surface may be reused right after) - left until the IOP copy row says the CPU copy matters; the per-frame re-upload of a small window surface (surface-in, a kernel import refused EPERM 217 times: surface 94) is a separate item. A bigger BAR0 (card run 2026-10-28c, RESIZE_BAR_LOG2=10): macOS 26.4.1 booted with BAR0 1 GiB, which OVMF put at 0x900000000 (its 64-bit window; QEMU's info pci at 8 and 40 s: still there), but both of the core's maps failed (DRM/amdgpu UAPI ready: VRAM 16368 MiB (0 MiB visible), cannot map BAR0 (VRAM aperture)): Stage 2 stopped, the framebuffer called the head outside BAR0, the accelerator's MM commit NOT MAPPED, WindowServer never reached the GPU (bootwatch). The console at phys 0x900000000 worked (EFI GOP). CAUSE (certain from Apple's IOPCIFamily-726.0.5 source, needs one card run to confirm on 26.4.1's binary): IOPCIConfigurator::addHostBridge clears kIOPCIConfiguratorPFM64 when CPUID says VMM (QEMU sets the hypervisor bit), so probeBaseAddressRegister leaves a 64-bit BAR's maxAddress at IOPCIRangeInit's 0xFFFFFFFF and IOPCIRangeListAllocateSubRange clamps every free range to it: in a VM a BAR above 4 GiB is never kept where the firmware put it, q35 has no free 1 GiB-aligned GiB below 4 GiB, so BAR0 gets no assigned-addresses entry and the IOPCIDevice no IODeviceMemory (getDeviceMemoryWithRegister NULL -> mapDeviceMemoryWithRegister NULL). Commit 0ae3df7 ("at 40 s macOS had them unmapped") was the same thing. Separately MAX_BAR_SIZE = 1 GiB: a bigger BAR's size is cut to its low 32 bits (16 GiB -> 0) on any machine: the "theoretical 1 GB maximum" of OpenCore's ResizeAppleGpuBars (Configuration.tex; ocvalidate calls values above 10 unsupported by macOS, above 8 unstable across sleep). NOT the cause: kernel VA (IOMemoryDescriptorMapAlloc maps into KMEM_RANGE_ID_DATA, which kmem_range_startup_init sizes to what is left of the kernel VA after the pointer ranges, tens of GiB on x86_64) or the cache mode. Apple's AMD driver maps windows of a BAR (AMDSupport ATIController::mapFramebuffer(bar, offset, length) -> mapPCISubRange: getDeviceMemoryWithRegister, IODeviceMemory::withSubRange, map(kIOMapAnywhere VERIFIED on the card, run 2026-10-28 22:29 (CI run 72, 29bb021, RESIZE_BAR_LOG2=9, no 64-bit PCI window): VRAM aperture: BAR0 512 MiB at phys 0x80000000, mapped 512 MiB ..., visible heap 400 MiB (15600 MiB beyond it), WindowServer on the accelerator. 1 GiB below 4 GiB is not expected to fit q35 (MMCONFIG at 0xB0000000); 1 GiB needs the -hypervisor experiment (open). | kIOMapWriteCombineCache); disassembled from 26.4.1's KC). (3) Core: rdna3IopSdma (rdna3k_abi.h RD3KIopSdma, ops BIND / COPY / UNBIND, statuses OK / NODEV / STALE / NOSPACE / INVALID / FAILED; RDNA3Accel::platformIopSdma under the DRM lock, RDNA3Bringup::iopGart*): an area of the GART nothing else uses (offset 4 MiB to 32 MiB under the top: above the IH rings, below the transfer window and the uf / uq / coherence slots), reset with every new GART table (hwInit: boot, GPU reset, wake; off in ipSuspend / stop) - log iop sdma (N63): GART area for the IOP copy at offset 4 MiB, X MiB, failures iop sdma (N63): op N failed ... (first 8, every 1024th). (4) Accelerator: the display pipe's source slots (4 per pipe, each keeps a surface's descriptor prepared and mapped) now also hold the surface's GART range: bound at the slot's first SDMA copy from the bus addresses of an IODMACommand (44-bit, mapped, as the core's gttWire; kept prepared while bound; a descriptor not starting on a page is left to the CPU), unbound in iopDropSlot BEFORE the descriptor is completed (slot reused, pipe freed); a STALE answer (new GART table) binds again once. Every frame rd3AccelIopBySdma asks the core to copy and returns after its fence: the transaction still ends with the frame in the scanout (N59's stale gate runs before it, as before). The first 256 SDMA copies, then every 16th, read 16 pixels of the scanout back through the BAR against the source. Any failure copies that frame with the CPU, counted by reason (core, pages, bind, copy, verify, off, scanout: SDMA writes a scanout only when its VRAM offset is the memory at its BAR address, BAR0's base learned from the core's own allocations - a framebuffer on the boot console without the core's head info reports offset 0 for a console that may lie elsewhere); three strikes (copy / verify / a failed bind) in a row switch the path off until the sysctl is written. Switch: boot-arg rdna3iopsdma / sysctl debug.rdna3accel_iop_sdma, DEFAULT 1 (the port reuses the GART + SDMA pattern the page-off and eviction run on the card since N54, a wrong picture is caught by the read-back and falls back by itself, a core without the function answers unsupported and keeps the CPU path). Counters debug.rdna3accel_iop_copy_sdma_{state,n,us,core_us,max_us,bytes,binds,bind_us,unbinds,ranges,verified,fb,fb_<reason>} (iop_copy_n/_us now count the CPU copies, iop_copy_{n,us}_h<i> both paths); logs iop sdma (N63): ... done by SDMA0 ... at start, iop: sdma bind #N, iop: sdma copy #N (head H): ... in U us (core C us, M MB/s), 16 samples read back match (first 4, every 1024th), iop: sdma -> CPU (head H, <reason> #k): ..., iop sdma (N63): switched OFF after 3 failures in a row, iop: sdma unbind #N. (path SDMA), SDMA avg ~1-2 ms (was 20 ms CPU), fallbacks to the CPU 0 (or a few core before the GART is up), frame order displayed fps well above 7.7 (still 0 regressions), no verify / copy fallbacks. OPEN: (a) the copy waits for its fence on WindowServer's thread under the core's DRM lock (~1-2 ms, other DRM ioctls wait behind it); a fence-signalled completion (isTransactionComplete) would free the thread but must keep the frame order; (b) ECC cpp-reviewer round 1: a fence timeout (status TIMEOUT) switches the path off at once (accelerator state 2) and the core takes no IOP copy until its next GART table; an unbind after it unmaps only once every fence on the ring passed (else range ... released but NOT unmapped), so a still-running copy never meets a pulled PTE - a late completion can still put one older frame over a CPU one; an unbind the core did not answer is retried before the next SDMA copy (16 kept), a NOSPACE / NODEV bind backs off 64 frames instead of walking the page list every frame; (c) the VRAM-resource source (fromRes, CPU reads through the BAR) could use rdna3CopyVram the same way if a run shows iop_src_res; (d) N61's zero-copy IOP flip would remove the copy altogether. What a WindowServer frame costs, framebuffer fetch made cheaper (card run 2026-10-29a, app survey after N63: displayed 11.3 fps of the page's 59.7; WindowServer census 340136 draws, 314275 of them framebuffer fetch, 6365 surface-in uploads / 4984 MB). FINDING (certain from the report): the low displayed rate is mostly the PROBE's. WindowServer's plugin trace (plugin_trace.py --rates, second 0 = 19:34:28 UTC) commits 122 command buffers a second (two per frame: 61 fps, in_upload 63/s) in seconds 518-529, 533-534, 542-543 and 571-577, and 20-36 a second (10-18 fps) in 535-570, exactly while the frame-order bursts ran (12:43:36 and 12:43:48 local = seconds 548 and 560, 10 s each) and the looks before them: rdna3_drminfo --scanout-strip read the scanout back to back (4864 samples in 10 s, ~2 ms per ioctl), each call holding the display lock (RDNA3Accel::drmScanout, fDispLock, also taken by displayFlip / displayCursor / displayScanAddr) while it reads 1280 pixels through BAR0. WHAT A FBFETCH DRAW COST (certain from code): one render pass carries the colour attachment as its own input attachment (GENERAL, by-region self-dependency), one descriptor set per pass, and before EVERY reading draw a global VkMemoryBarrier COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT/WRITE -> FRAGMENT_SHADER/INPUT_ATTACHMENT_READ, BY_REGION (nvmtl_fbf_bind); no render-pass split and no copy. In RADV (Mesa d381c361 radv_cmd_buffer.c radv_src_access_flush / radv_dst_access_flush / radv_stage_flush) a barrier without an image has image_is_coherent false and has_CB_meta true: CB flush + CB metadata flush + PS partial flush + a whole-GL2 invalidate per draw. Measured on the host's RX 7800 XT (RADV 26.2.5, tests/vk_fbfetch_probe.c case 5: 400 overlapping window-tile fetch draws into BGRA8 1920x1080): 1.456 ms with the memory barrier, 1.335 ms with an image barrier, 0.323 ms with none (2.8 / 2.5 us a draw): ~1 ms of GPU a WindowServer frame, not the cap on smoothness. No cheaper ordering exists on this hardware/driver: RADV offers neither VK_EXT_ nor VK_ARM_rasterization_order_attachment_access (dev host vulkaninfo); VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering_local_read needs the same barrier; VK_EXT_attachment_feedback_loop_layout keeps DCC decompressed (radv_layout_dcc_compressed). DONE: (1) the barrier is a VkImageMemoryBarrier on each attachment the draw reads (GENERAL -> GENERAL, its level / slice; legal inside the pass, VUID-vkCmdPipelineBarrier-image-04073), so RADV skips the GL2 invalidate (radv_image_is_l2_coherent); nvmtl_fbf_barrier, decision nvmtl_fbf_barrier_mode (nvmtl_fbfetch_plan.h, test_fbfetch_plan); A/B NVMTL_FBF_MEMBAR=1 restores the old barrier (in nvmtl_ab_knobs); vk: fbfetch: SUMMARY ... bar_img= bar_mem= bar_noimg=, log got the OLD global barrier for a draw whose attachment has no image; the probe's ordering cases (40 overlapping RAW draws, 60 interleaved RAW/WAR draws, restarts, sRGB/UNORM/F16/F32) pass with both barriers on the host. (2) vk: ws frame: SUMMARY (nvmtl_ws_frame.h, test_ws_frame): per command buffer that drew, encode (begin -> submit), GPU (its TOP/BOTTOM timestamps), fence wait, passes, draws, fbfetch draws and barriers by kind, surface-in uploads, frames per active second and the busiest second; triage row ws frame (triage_present_cost.py). (3) rdna3_drminfo --scanout-strip sleeps after each round as long as it took (50 % duty, at most 8 ms; # pacing: line), triage frame order says probe paced / probe UNPACED. NEXT RUN must show: frame order displayed fps near the page's with probe paced; ws frame WindowServer busiest second ~122, encode / GPU per frame, image barriers only; glyphs (Terminal) and Chess still clean. OPEN: if the paced burst still drops WindowServer, shorten the kernel's lock hold (read only the strip's 432 columns: a ncols input to RD_NR_XNU_SCANOUT) or read the scanout without fDispLock; the surface-in uploads (63/s, avg 2.3 ms, synchronous on WindowServer's encoding thread) are the next CPU cost (the surf-in cache N51 already skips unchanged surfaces; the window surface whose kernel import is refused EPERM stays a copy); removing the fbfetch barrier entirely would need raster-order access RADV does not offer, or a fragment-shader-interlock (POPS) storage-image path in the translator (big, ~1 ms/frame to gain). | VERIFIED on the card, run 2026-10-29 13:44 (plugin with the image barriers, kernel N63): WindowServer composed 38442 frames in 609 active seconds = 63.1 frames/s, avg 4.04 ms a frame (encode 3.56, GPU 0.49), 17.5 framebuffer-fetch draws a frame all with image barriers (OLD memory 0); Terminal glyphs CLEAN 18/18; 15 of 15 apps ran. A Metal game on bare metal renders ~190 fps where Linux/RADV gives ~2000 (2026-10-31, wgpu Minecraft client horizon-client, VSync off; rdna3_pmctl: gfx 100 % at 2229 MHz; render passes average 69 us). DTrace over 3 s: presentDrawable 560, commit 7472, -[NVMTLTexture nvmtlSurfaceOutDMA] 2800 on exactly 3 texture objects (the layer's 3 drawables): ~5 full 1920x1080 BGRA copy-outs per frame (8.3 MB each into the IOSurface's host pages over PCIe, present cost: SUMMARY ... gpu_us_avg=444), ~35 MB and ~2 ms of GPU per frame. FINDING (certain from code): the game writes its drawable in several command buffers per frame, and every seal (nvmtl_seal_surfaces, NVMTLEventSync.m) recorded a copy-out of every surface in _surfDirty and -nvmtlAdvancePart published each one; only the copy before the present is read (WindowServer reads the surface after the present). DONE: a root texture handed to a present call gets _soDrawable; the command buffer keeps the roots it presents (_presentRoots, all of them, not only _present); the seal leaves out of part->surfaces every _soDrawable root this command buffer does not present (nvmtl_drawable_defer, nvmtl_drawable_defer.h, tests/test_drawable_defer.c), so the writes stay in the private image, ordered on the one GPU queue before the presenting buffer's copy-out; surface-in compares seeds and the seed does not move for a deferred part, so a later Load pass does not upload stale bytes. Kill switch NVMTL_DRAWABLE_DEFER=0 (default ON; in nvmtl_ab_knobs); logs commit: N69 DRAWABLE_DEFER ON|off ... once and commit: N69 <n> copy-outs of drawable surfaces deferred to their present (NVMTL_DRAWABLE_DEFER) at powers of two, then every 65536. NEXT MAC RUN must show: DTrace nvmtlSurfaceOutDMA count ~ presentDrawable count; present cost: SUMMARY outs ~ frames; fps up; no black or stale content in the game, Terminal or other apps (apps that never present a drawable are untouched: the flag is only set by a present call). OPEN: an app that reads its drawable's IOSurface on the CPU (or another process through the surface) before presenting it, or that presents a drawable it never writes in the presenting buffer, sees the older IOSurface contents until the present (compare with NVMTL_DRAWABLE_DEFER=0); a drawable written and then dropped without a present is never copied out; an app that mixes presentDrawable: with [drawable present] (no nvmtlRecordDrawable call) gets no deferral and keeps the old per-buffer copy-outs; _soDrawable is a plain BOOL set on the encoding thread and read at seal (benign race); a CPU/WindowServer write that moves the surface seed between a deferred write and a Load pass would make surface-in upload the surface over the newer private image.

N70 done, card open

The G-SUB submit-ahead pass stopped at every command buffer's empty last part (bare metal 2026-10-31, horizon-client after N69, docs/BAREMETAL-REMOTE.html round 2 item 6: DTrace over 3 s 7343 commits, 3437 submits, 3396 of them nvmtl_vk_submit_wait_at (submit + fence wait in turn), 35 nvmtl_gsub_finish; 840 of 1059 nvmtlSubmitAhead calls ended at the empty-part test and nvmtl_gsub_ahead_pass1 breaks at the first NO). FINDING (certain from code, -[NVMTLCommandBuffer nvmtlSubmitAhead]): an empty split part (no encoder / resource / surface / scratch) was retired ahead only when it was not the buffer's last part, but every buffer that ends after an event split has an empty last part, so the pass returned NO there and no later buffer was submitted ahead. DONE (re-applied on the code after N73 from 4436584; host test, needs the card): the last empty part is retired like the others (nvk_cmdbuf abandoned, aheadEmpty, its events promised, the buffer marked scheduled: all its parts are in flight) and the pass goes on to the next buffer; nvmtlAdvancePart then finds commands.cb NULL and only signals and finishes. The decision is common-style pure C in metal/plugin/nvmtl_gsub_empty.h (nvmtl_gsub_empty_verdict), tests/test_gsub_empty.c. Ordering is unchanged (checked on the N69/N73 code): presents and the drawable completion run in nvmtlFinishExecution, after nvmtlAdvancePart has published every part, and a part with a copy-out has surfaces (never empty) or dma (held by the N61 test before); a presenting buffer's last part is therefore never retired empty ahead. Kill switch NVMTL_GSUB_LAST_EMPTY=0 (old stop; in nvmtl_ab_knobs; logs commit: N70 G-SUB an empty LAST part is retired by the ahead pass, which goes on to the next buffer|stops the ahead pass (...) once). Counters (log lines at powers of two): commit: N70 G-SUB <n> empty LAST parts retired by the ahead pass (the pass went on to the next buffer), ... <n> ahead pass(es) stopped at an empty LAST part (NVMTL_GSUB_LAST_EMPTY=0), ... <n> ahead pass(es) stopped at an empty part (NVMTL_EVFOLD=0); the existing commit: sub1 EVFOLD N empty split parts retired by the ahead pass and the N61 part(s) with a GPU copy into an IOSurface ... NOT submitted ahead lines are unchanged. NEXT CARD/MAC RUN (horizon-client, same DTrace as BAREMETAL-REMOTE round 2): most submits through nvmtl_gsub_finish, fence-wait share and the plugin execution thread's CPU down (was 2.6 s -> 0.68 s per 4 s with 4436584), N70 ... empty LAST parts retired growing with the commits, no stale or missing frames; fps may not move (WindowServer's upload caps the game) - A/B with NVMTL_GSUB_LAST_EMPTY=0. OPEN: the N61 dma part of the presenting buffer still stops the pass once per frame; the empty-part retire promises a part's signals before the CPU sets them (as for non-last parts), fine while all parts run on the one GPU queue.

WindowServer cannot import another process's family-VRAM IOSurface (bare metal 2026-10-31 after BAR0 1 GiB: item 7: surface N plane 0: family VRAM import refused (VkResult -1000072003, kernel errno 1 Operation not permitted, 8294400 B)). Effect: an app's drawable placed in VRAM (item 7, copy-out "dirty, no copy") is read by WindowServer's surface-in through the BAR with the CPU (~4.5 ms a 1080p frame, sin_us ~57 % of ws_main_thread, WS 187 % CPU, horizon-client 123-145 presented fps); with NVMTL_NO_SURFACE_SHARE=1 in the app (private image + N61 DMA copy-out) ~215 fps and WS 80 %. FOUND (certain from code, 4b9b2c9): not an ownership check: nvmtl_vk_surface_vram -> drm_xnu_surface_fd / surface_fd_to_handle (RD_NR_XNU_SURFACE_BO IMPORT) -> uapi surface_bo -> kext drmSurfaceBo (RD3K_ESC_SURF_GEM) -> accel rd3AccelSurfGem: lookupSurface succeeds, rd3AccelA3Policy refuses (proc WindowServer + the size of a head's scanout/swap -> RD3_A3_REFUSE_DISPLAY -> RD3K_SS_DISPLAY -> -EPERM; any other WindowServer import -> RD3_A3_REFUSE_WS -> RD3K_SS_POLICY -> -EPERM); a3ws=1 alone would not fix the display-size case. The rule exists because in card run 58 / 58b (aa913df, 751f5b8, ac1a6db) WindowServer WROTE into imported shared VRAM (its CPU surface-in uploaded stale system memory over what other processes drew); reading shared VRAM was never the problem. Also: N70 (quick/gsub-last) not merged, see BAREMETAL-REMOTE round 2 item 6. PLAN (c) (owner-approved): an app's VRAM drawable is imported by WindowServer READ-ONLY and sampled directly: no GPU copy-out into the IOSurface's pages, no CPU upload in WindowServer; WindowServer never writes into it, so the card run 58 mechanism (a reader writing shared VRAM) cannot occur. RISKS and their protections (each with its log line / counter and test): R1 WindowServer still sampling while the producer writes the next frame into the same drawable (read-after-write): the producer presents only after its GPU finished the presenting buffer and WindowServer commits with waitUntilCompleted (NVMTLObjects.m :5922-5927), but nothing stops the producer from starting the NEXT frame into the same drawable while WindowServer's sample is in flight. Protection: a detector in WindowServer (step 4): the producer's tag generation (nvmtl_vram_tag_bump seed) recorded at WindowServer's commit and compared at its completion; a change = a hazard, counted sh_war in the plugin's present cost SUMMARY and logged (vk: a3share: surface S hazard #n: tag G0 at commit, G1 at completion), and automatic self-protection: a surface with a hazard is demoted to the private copy path for the rest of its life (logged once per surface), after 4 hazards in the session WindowServer stops shared imports altogether (logged once, vk: a3share: OFF for this session after 4 hazards). A fence-based fix (the producer waits for WindowServer's read) is OPEN and only if the card shows hazards. RW write) and markGuilty names the client and pid, but not the BO; added: the faulting client's next refused submission (CS on a guilty VM) or context query looks the fault VA up in its own mappings, and a hit in a shared read-only BO is reported to the accelerator, which turns sharing off (debug.rdna3accel_a3share=0) and logs surfshare: VM fault at VA 0x... in a read-only shared import of surface S plane P (importer pid X, producer pid Y): sharing turned OFF (a3share_fault_off n), counter debug.rdna3accel_a3share_fault_off. SECURITY REVIEW FOLLOW-UP (ecc:security-reviewer over 6b3c35d..b9b4c72: no CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM): (LOW-1, version skew) the read-only import is OPT-IN: the plugin calls drm_xnu_surface_accept_ro(1) (not with NVMTL_WS_SHARE=0), libdrm_xnu then sets RD_XNU_SURFACE_BO_IN_ACCEPT_RO in the IMPORT request's tail, the uapi passes RD_SURFACE_BO_ACCEPT_RO to ops.surface_bo, the core sends RD3K_SG_ACCEPT_RO and the accelerator looks for a share only then; without it (an older plugin still loaded in a WindowServer that was not restarted after an update, whose writable alias would fault and take the desktop down) the import is refused exactly as before (log surfshare: WindowServer's import of surface S plane P does not accept a read-only one), and the uapi refuses an OS answer of gpu_ro to a caller that did not accept it (-EPERM, its hold given back); tests: tests/test_uapi.c (zero tail, 24-byte struct, forced gpu_ro: -EPERM; IN_ACCEPT_RO: SHARED_RO), test_libdrm_amdgpu. (LOW-2) R1 hazards are counted per PRODUCER: the import tail (out_ver 2) carries the producer's pid (share_pid, drm_xnu_surface_last_import_pid, nvk_buffer share_pid), 4 hazards of one producer demote that producer (vk: a3share: producer pid P demoted after 4 hazards, once; sh_pdem; none of its surfaces is imported read-only, the others keep theirs), the session stop is only a backstop at 64 hazards of all producers; hazard lines are rate limited per producer (its first 4, then every 64th); tests in tests/test_ws_share.c. OPEN from the review: (LOW-3) WindowServer's read-only imports are charged to its own per-file foreign-bytes cap RD_SURFACE_BO_FILE_MAX (half the visible VRAM): many shared drawables give it -ENOSPC (R4, the private path); a separate budget for read-only imports or charging the producer is open; (note) op 4 (the share record) takes the accelerator's family lock like DIRTY, once per (surface, generation) - watch the lock traffic on the card; (note) alloc_gen and share_pid are exposed to the importer in the tail (a generation counter and another process's pid, both of a surface the caller may already look up). A producer demoted after 4 hazards (sh_pdem, vk: a3share: producer pid N demoted ...) stays demoted for the whole WindowServer session and pids are reused, so an unrelated later application with that pid is kept on the private path too (performance only; the table keeps a demoted entry, a full table forgets a not-demoted producer first); a producer's cached read-only aliases are dropped at the next wrapper (nvmtl_wss_plan_demote), aliases already handed out live on until WindowServer lets them go. A demoted surface ring of 128 can still be churned by an application that puts bogus NVMTLShareGen values on many surfaces (each failing import demotes its surface, an evicted genuine demotion gets one more chance; a demoted producer stays demoted whatever the ring holds). STILL OPEN: step 5 (CI full: true build of the plugin, the card round); R1's fence-based fix only if the card shows hazards; the fault attribution runs only at the faulting client's next refused submission or context query (a WindowServer that never submits again after its VM was marked guilty is not attributed: the VA is still in the core's [gfxhub] page fault line).

FINDING (certain from code, reproduced with hand-made fixtures, no Apple IR): %metal2vulkan.ct.slot.N is the construct tree's dispatch-loop slot (src/native/cfg/structured_emit/construct_tree/renest.rs, whole-CFG dispatch; ct.sl.slot.N is the straddle one), not the function-constant path (that line is only the plugin's re-translate after specialization): slot.N = phi [undef, pre], [nextslot.N, continue], nextslot.N merges each case's Function or Private value with slot.N itself. N72 split a phi only from incoming values already emitted, and a back-edge value never is: the slot (and, in raw construction, the original loop phi whose back-edge value %1425 is a Function/Private phi emitted after it) fell through to the old paths. DONE (metal/translator): src/native/emitter/pointers/mixed_network.rs works out, per function before emission, the storages every address-space-0 pointer can come from (alloca Function, address-space-0 global Private, GEPs/copies their base's, phis the union, anything else unknown; worklist, linear); a value with exactly {Function, Private} is a network member, and all members are split the same way under names fixed in advance (arm <v>.metal2vulkan.mixarm<g>, a copy shares its source's; selector <phi>.metal2vulkan.mixarm.cond, GEPs/copies use their base's), with the arm phis and arm GEPs registered as forward TIR facts so the index-domain machinery resolves a forward arm. emit_mixed_storage_pointer_phi (phi_mixed_storage.rs) takes a forward member (its arms and selector), a forward value of one storage (its arm as it is; the other arm's zero from the nearest value of that storage in the network), undef (undef on both arms); members are split before the selected-access-tree path can take them (phi_compare.rs). A walk over a split pointer is now split too (N72's refusal of it is gone); a select of one, or a phi that also merges an unknown pointer, is still refused by name; a member that cannot be split after a phi took its arms is refused (... cannot be split, but a phi already took <arm>; this network is not supported (N75)), an arm that cannot take its zero path likewise (... arm's index domain cannot take the arm's structural zero path (N75), ... arm has no value to take a structural zero path from (N75)), never a module with an undefined id. ECC review of 14081f6 (rust-reviewer, HIGH-1: a network with two roots of one storage translated reading the first root only): the analysis also tracks each value's roots (allocas, Private globals; capped at 3), and a member phi whose inflow has more than one root of a storage is refused (mixed-storage pointer phi <p>: its network has more than one <Function|Private> root (<a>, <b>) (N75)); a phi taking a member that was emitted without its split is refused (... takes member <m>, which was emitted without its split; this network is not supported (N75)); a phi or select outside the network taking a copy of a member is refused like the member itself. Also fixed by it: a loop phi with a Function entry value and a forward Private-only phi on the back edge translated and validated but addressed the alloca on every iteration (the Private table was never read). Navi 33 / RX 7600 XT 的 SMU 子阶段按真实 MP1 discovery 分派：13.0.7 使用独立 smu13_0_7.c 与 Linux v6.12 的 IF 0x35、PPTable / BoardTable / Metrics ABI；13.0.0/10 保留原 vendor 布局与 PM 行为。保留 flags 0x03b10028 与 MP1 segment 0 检查；未知硬件不默认 Navi 32。13.0.7 bootstrap 读取 combo，校验真实布局、范围与 IF，非 SCPM 必须从有效 VBIOS 追加完整 opaque BoardTable 并上传 PPTable；SCPM 不拼板表、不上传、不写 allowed mask。allowed features 从零按 13.0.7 算法构造，再应用调用者的功能限制；使能后读取 DPM、按实际 PCIe caps 更新、配置 thermal alert，基础频率 / 温度 / 功率 / 风扇转速与 INFO 经独立 metrics 解码。不发送未注册回调的 RunDcBtc / DALNotPresent，不移植 Navi 32 自定义的 SCPM UCLK/FCLK 顶频策略。独立 DAL mailbox 的 hard-min status 门槛按 GC 分派，DCN321 跳过 FCLK 通知。clock read 按 GC 11.0.2 选择 321 的 0x16e64 DFS 组，11.0.0/3 保留 320 的 0x16e69；未知 GC 在寄存器读取前失败，所有实际 caller 消费返回值。其余 DCN321 显示寄存器仍需独立移植。stats / sensor 共用有效性检查与真实刷新缓存，失败 INFO 不会把旧值标成新鲜。当前没有真实 OS 供电输入，7 不强制 NotifyPowerSource(AC)；初始当前 PPT 限制未知，UINT32_MAX 表示不可用，不影响独立实际功率 metrics / INFO。真正的 FW AC/DC 事件可更新固件上下文；这不是完整 OS late-init 电源策略。高级 OD、手动风扇、功率限制写、BACO 与性能级编辑仍不支持；13.0.7 reset 使用 Linux 共用 debug mailbox，fini/shutdown 仅清理 thermal alert，不清 flags 或盲发 unload / DisableAll。test_smu13 执行生产 C 与独立字节 fixture，覆盖成功、错误输入、错误响应、ABI 隔离、DAL 调用链与生命周期；sim_navi32 是原路径回归，不是 Navi 33 实卡证据。新增 C 与 native 编译仍须本派生的 GitHub hosted CI 实际验证。缺少本卡 Linux discovery、VBIOS、SCPM / PMFW 返回、DCN321 其余移植和受控实卡结果；7480 IOKit 匹配保持禁用，没有宣称 Sequoia 出图、Metal 或显卡完成。

Left out on purpose (project decisions): HDCP / secure display TAs, eDP / PSR / ABM, USB-C / DPIA, KFD / ROCm, RAS / ECC, XGMI.

How much can be written without the card

  • Simulator-verifiable ports (register sequences from named amdgpu functions: P1, P4, S1-S3, S5, S6, A2, V1, R1, R2, R5) can be written in large batches - several thousand lines before a card run - because the simulator checks the order and values against amdgpu. What stays unknown is how the card reacts; each batch therefore ships behind a boot-arg and the round's single big step turns all of them on, with variant fallbacks in code (as the accelerator's head strategies did).
  • Behaviour-dependent work (P2, S2's exact clock path, A3, M2-M6, V3) depends on what macOS / the card do and cannot be written far ahead: one round per discovery. Here the gain is in making each round report more (pictures, power trend, mtlprobe, CTS) so no round is spent on a question the report could have answered.

Test rounds

One boot per round (rdna3vm queue pending, step big), unattended, then rdna3vm result. The report already holds: DMA/GFX tests, Vulkan, PM trend, accelerator swap/flip log, and (from 944d983) a picture of what each display pipe scans out. Planned additions, each cheap: mtlprobe (M1), CTS subset (V2), hang test (R1), soak (R2), mode switching with a picture per mode (S4).

Order

  1. A1 (pictures confirm two heads) -> defaults on (A1, P3).
  2. P1 + P2 diagnostics (idle power), S1 + S5 + S6, V1, R1 - one batch, one boot.
  3. M1 (does Metal see the card at all?) - early, because it sizes finish line M.
  4. S2-S4 (mode setting), A2.
  5. P4/P5 (sleep), R2, R3 (bare metal).
  6. M2 -> M6 iteratively; V2/V3 alongside.
  7. R4, R5; R6 only if everything else holds.

D4: display state across resets and sleep (2026-10-10, sim/host verified, step sleep)

The S3 gap - what a full resume still lacks, in the order it will bite:

  1. Suspend side. Done (a3b2ec6, rdna3resume=1): systemSuspend runs amdgpu_fence_driver_hw_fini and amdgpu_device_ip_suspend_phase2 (VCN/JPEG, MES, SDMA, GFX, PSP, IH, GMC, SoC); every wake then runs the resume. smu_suspend has nothing to send on 13.0.7 (mp1_state NONE).
  2. VRAM contents. amdgpu evicts every VRAM BO to system memory before the sleep (amdgpu_device_evict_resources) and moves them back; here VRAM is simply lost: user BOs, the accelerator's and the framebuffer's surfaces (black until WindowServer redraws), and the GPUVM page-table pool - live VMs get their MMHUB/GFXHUB contexts back from fVmPd but the tables behind them are garbage, so old contexts fault (clients see VRAM lost). Page tables: done - after a VRAM loss the pool starts over, each live VM gets a new root and the UAPI maps every client's ranges again (rdna3_drm_device_vm_replay). BO contents: done - every DRM BO in VRAM, the seq64 slots, the accelerator's surfaces and the boot scanout are copied to wired system pages through SDMA before the suspend and back after a power loss (kext/src/RDNA3Evict.cpp).
  3. Display init. dcn32_init_hw is not ported: the clock manager's raise half runs in the restore now (DISPCLK / DPPCLK hard minimums through the DAL mailbox, back to the saved clocks; restored (-16) only when that fails or DISPCLK sits at divider 127); DCFCLK and the rest of dcn32_init_clocks are not ported; DIO / PHY init of the unused links; the replay of the saved DCHUBBUB / DCCG globals stands in for hubbub32_init, dccg32_init and hubbub3_init_dchub_sys_ctx.
  4. Lost display contents. The gamma / degamma LUT RAMs (a restore after a power loss sets them to bypass; the table macOS last sent is loaded again now, displayGammaReload), the VPG infoframe contents (infoframes off, as after a mode set), HDMI audio.
  5. DMCUB. rdna3dmub=3 attaches to the GOP's firmware; after a power loss or a mode-1 reset that firmware is gone and the re-init now loads our own, as rdna3dmub=1 does (RDNA3,DMUBReloaded).
  6. DP heads. Done for DP SST on one pipe with rdna3dplink=1: the sleep takes the link down (link_set_dpms_off), the restore brings the stream resources back (the link cap verified for a new sink, DTBCLK again for 128b/132b) and trains the link again (link_set_dpms_on); monitor standby goes through the link too. DP MST and ODM combined heads are still refused (blank only).
  7. Ordering with macOS. The wake work runs on a thread call after setPowerState(1); framebuffer power requests that arrive earlier are recorded and applied afterwards. A picture lost in VRAM (a wake or a mode-1 reset whose VRAM did not all come back) moves RD3KDisplayHotplug.lost; every framebuffer then raises its connect-change interrupt, so IOGraphics probes the head again and WindowServer draws everything again (RDNA3,DisplayPictureLost, RDNA3,PictureLost on the framebuffer).
  8. Hotplug across the sleep. Done: the HPD looks stop before the sleep (amdgpu_dm_irq_suspend; after a power loss the pins read unplugged until they are enabled again), the wake work programs the HPD pins and filters again (link_resume) and the looks start after the longest connect filter; a monitor plugged or pulled while asleep counts as a hotplug then.
